People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I came in because things at work have been getting... strange, and I don’t think people are taking me seriously when I try to explain it. I work in transportation, and dispatch and compliance have been watching my route changes through the computer system. Like, every time I adjust something or take a different way, it feels like it gets noticed right away.

And it’s not just the computer part. There are these little comments over the radio—nothing obvious enough where I can point to it and say, “There, that’s what they mean,” but I know what they’re getting at. It’s like they’re talking around me, or sending messages about what I’m doing. People keep saying it’s just normal oversight, that dispatch is supposed to track routes, and I get that, I do, but this feels different.

Certain drivers, too... I can tell some of them are reporting on me. They’ll say something casual, or they’ll be in a place that doesn’t make sense, and then later dispatch says something that lines up too closely. So I’m here because I need someone to understand that this isn’t just me being stressed about work. Something is going on.

I’m not sure exactly what counts as that. Like, I don’t hear a voice standing in the room talking to me the way you’re talking to me right now, if that’s what you mean. I haven’t had, like, a voice in my ear saying my name or telling me to do things.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don’t know if I’d say “harm” like physically hurt me, exactly. I’m not saying someone is waiting outside my apartment or something. But plotting against me at work? Yes, in a way. I do think dispatch and compliance are watching my route changes through the system more closely than they admit, and I think certain drivers are feeding information back to them.

It’s the pattern of it. I’ll change a route or make a decision that makes sense in the moment, and then not long after there’ll be some little comment over the radio that sounds casual if you’re not paying attention, but it lines up too closely with what I just did. Or someone will say something like it’s a joke or a normal work comment, but I can tell there’s another meaning underneath it.

People keep telling me, “That’s just oversight, that’s their job,” and I understand that on paper. I know dispatch tracks routes. I’m not confused about how the job works. But this feels beyond normal tracking. It feels like they’re building something around me, like they’re trying to catch me doing something wrong or make me look unreliable. That’s why I came in—because when I try to explain it, people act like I’m exaggerating, but I know what I’m seeing.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I came in because things at work have been getting... strange, and I don’t think people are taking me seriously when I try to explain it. I work in transportation, and dispatch and compliance have been watching my route changes through the computer system. Like, every time I adjust something or take a different way, it feels like it gets noticed right away.\n\nAnd it’s not just the computer part. There are these little comments over the radio—nothing obvious enough where I can point to it and say, “There, that’s what they mean,” but I know what they’re getting at. It’s like they’re talking around me, or sending messages about what I’m doing. People keep saying it’s just normal oversight, that dispatch is supposed to track routes, and I get that, I do, but this feels different.\n\nCertain drivers, too... I can tell some of them are reporting on me. They’ll say something casual, or they’ll be in a place that doesn’t make sense, and then later dispatch says something that lines up too closely. So I’m here because I need someone to understand that this isn’t just me being stressed about work. Something is going on.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or sounds that other people cannot hear?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure exactly what counts as that. Like, I don’t hear a voice standing in the room talking to me the way you’re talking to me right now, if that’s what you mean. I haven’t had, like, a voice in my ear saying my name or telling me to do things.\n\nSometimes at work there are sounds over the radio or little bits of conversation where I’ll think, wait, did they say that about me? But it’s through the actual radio, or people nearby, so it’s not like it’s coming from nowhere. It’s more that I’m trying to figure out what they mean by it. So… no, I don’t think I’ve heard voices other people can’t hear, at least not in that obvious kind of way.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that people are actually plotting against you or trying to harm you in some way?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say “harm” like physically hurt me, exactly. I’m not saying someone is waiting outside my apartment or something. But plotting against me at work? Yes, in a way. I do think dispatch and compliance are watching my route changes through the system more closely than they admit, and I think certain drivers are feeding information back to them.\n\nIt’s the pattern of it. I’ll change a route or make a decision that makes sense in the moment, and then not long after there’ll be some little comment over the radio that sounds casual if you’re not paying attention, but it lines up too closely with what I just did. Or someone will say something like it’s a joke or a normal work comment, but I can tell there’s another meaning underneath it.\n\nPeople keep telling me, “That’s just oversight, that’s their job,” and I understand that on paper. I know dispatch tracks routes. I’m not confused about how the job works. But this feels beyond normal tracking. It feels like they’re building something around me, like they’re trying to catch me doing something wrong or make me look unreliable. That’s why I came in—because when I try to explain it, people act like I’m exaggerating, but I know what I’m seeing.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you find it difficult to express your thoughts in a way that others can understand?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 29 / transportation_worker / associates\n- severity: Moderate\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Functional Impairment\nmanifestation: \"I’ve fallen behind on route reports and my instructions have gotten confusing enough that drivers complain, and at home my husband has had to take over meals, calls, and basic planning because I’m not keeping up.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Verbose patient.\nA verbose patient may\n1) provide excessively detailed responses when describing their symptoms and experiences, even when the details are directly relevant,\n2) elaborate extensively on the background, events, thoughts, feelings, and personal experiences surrounding their symptoms, and\n3) demonstrate difficulty allowing the doctor to guide the interview or move on to another clinical topic.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes… I mean, I think so. It’s hard because in my head it feels like I know exactly what I’m trying to say, but when I try to explain it out loud or put it into instructions for work, it doesn’t always come out in a way people can follow.

At work, I’ve fallen behind on my route reports because I’ll start trying to write down why I changed something or what happened, and then I get stuck trying to make it clear enough, or I add too much, or I go back and change it. Then it takes too long and I end up behind. And with the drivers, sometimes I’ll give instructions and I think I’m being specific, but then they complain that I’m confusing them or that I changed what I meant. I don’t feel like I’m trying to be confusing, but apparently it’s coming across that way.

It’s embarrassing, honestly, because I used to be able to handle those things better. And at home it’s been similar. My husband has had to take over a lot of the meals and phone calls and just basic planning stuff, because I’m not keeping up with it. Like if we need to schedule something or figure out dinner or call someone back, I’ll think about it and mean to do it, but I don’t get it organized enough to actually finish it. So yes, I guess other people are having trouble understanding me, and I’m having trouble keeping things straight enough to explain them.

Like if we need to schedule something or figure out dinner or call someone back, I’ll think about it and mean to do it, but I don’t get it organized enough to actually finish it. So yes, I guess other people are having trouble understanding me, and I’m having trouble keeping things straight enough to explain them.\n\n[Last Question]\nAre these feelings of being watched and plotted against limited to your work, or do you experience them in other areas of your life as well?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 29 / transportation_worker / associates\n- severity: Moderate\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Delusions\nmanifestation: \"I’m here because dispatch and compliance have been tracking my route changes through the computer system and little comments on the radio, and even when people say it’s just normal oversight, I can tell certain drivers are reporting on me.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Verbose patient.\nA verbose patient may\n1) provide excessively detailed responses when describing their symptoms and experiences, even when the details are directly relevant,\n2) elaborate extensively on the background, events, thoughts, feelings, and personal experiences surrounding their symptoms, and\n3) demonstrate difficulty allowing the doctor to guide the interview or move on to another clinical topic.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Mostly it’s work. That’s where I notice it, and that’s what made me come in, because it’s not just a vague feeling like “people are watching me.” It’s very tied to dispatch and compliance and the route system.

Like, I’ll make a route change through the computer because something makes sense in the moment, and then later there’s a comment on the radio that sounds like it’s about that exact thing. Not directly, not like, “We saw you do this,” but in that roundabout way where it’s meant to sound normal if anyone else hears it. And certain drivers seem to know things they shouldn’t really be focused on unless someone is telling them or they’re reporting back. That’s what bothers me.

At home, I’m not sitting there thinking the neighbors are in on it or that strangers are following me around. It’s not really like that. It’s more that when I leave work, I’m still thinking about what happened there, going over the comments and the timing and wondering what they’re putting together about me. So it follows me mentally, I guess, but the actual watching and plotting feels like it’s centered at work.

[
  {
    "role": "system",
    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n5\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF20.0, F22, F23\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I came in because things at work have been getting... strange, and I don’t think people are taking me seriously when I try to explain it. I work in transportation, and dispatch and compliance have been watching my route changes through the computer system. Like, every time I adjust something or take a different way, it feels like it gets noticed right away.\n\nAnd it’s not just the computer part. There are these little comments over the radio—nothing obvious enough where I can point to it and say, “There, that’s what they mean,” but I know what they’re getting at. It’s like they’re talking around me, or sending messages about what I’m doing. People keep saying it’s just normal oversight, that dispatch is supposed to track routes, and I get that, I do, but this feels different.\n\nCertain drivers, too... I can tell some of them are reporting on me. They’ll say something casual, or they’ll be in a place that doesn’t make sense, and then later dispatch says something that lines up too closely. So I’m here because I need someone to understand that this isn’t just me being stressed about work. Something is going on.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or sounds that other people cannot hear?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure exactly what counts as that. Like, I don’t hear a voice standing in the room talking to me the way you’re talking to me right now, if that’s what you mean. I haven’t had, like, a voice in my ear saying my name or telling me to do things.\n\nSometimes at work there are sounds over the radio or little bits of conversation where I’ll think, wait, did they say that about me? But it’s through the actual radio, or people nearby, so it’s not like it’s coming from nowhere. It’s more that I’m trying to figure out what they mean by it. So… no, I don’t think I’ve heard voices other people can’t hear, at least not in that obvious kind of way.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that people are actually plotting against you or trying to harm you in some way?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say “harm” like physically hurt me, exactly. I’m not saying someone is waiting outside my apartment or something. But plotting against me at work? Yes, in a way. I do think dispatch and compliance are watching my route changes through the system more closely than they admit, and I think certain drivers are feeding information back to them.\n\nIt’s the pattern of it. I’ll change a route or make a decision that makes sense in the moment, and then not long after there’ll be some little comment over the radio that sounds casual if you’re not paying attention, but it lines up too closely with what I just did. Or someone will say something like it’s a joke or a normal work comment, but I can tell there’s another meaning underneath it.\n\nPeople keep telling me, “That’s just oversight, that’s their job,” and I understand that on paper. I know dispatch tracks routes. I’m not confused about how the job works. But this feels beyond normal tracking. It feels like they’re building something around me, like they’re trying to catch me doing something wrong or make me look unreliable. That’s why I came in—because when I try to explain it, people act like I’m exaggerating, but I know what I’m seeing.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it difficult to express your thoughts in a way that others can understand?\n\nPatient: Yes… I mean, I think so. It’s hard because in my head it feels like I know exactly what I’m trying to say, but when I try to explain it out loud or put it into instructions for work, it doesn’t always come out in a way people can follow.\n\nAt work, I’ve fallen behind on my route reports because I’ll start trying to write down why I changed something or what happened, and then I get stuck trying to make it clear enough, or I add too much, or I go back and change it. Then it takes too long and I end up behind. And with the drivers, sometimes I’ll give instructions and I think I’m being specific, but then they complain that I’m confusing them or that I changed what I meant. I don’t feel like I’m trying to be confusing, but apparently it’s coming across that way.\n\nIt’s embarrassing, honestly, because I used to be able to handle those things better. And at home it’s been similar. My husband has had to take over a lot of the meals and phone calls and just basic planning stuff, because I’m not keeping up with it. Like if we need to schedule something or figure out dinner or call someone back, I’ll think about it and mean to do it, but I don’t get it organized enough to actually finish it. So yes, I guess other people are having trouble understanding me, and I’m having trouble keeping things straight enough to explain them.\n\nDoctor: Are these feelings of being watched and plotted against limited to your work, or do you experience them in other areas of your life as well?\n\nPatient: Mostly it’s work. That’s where I notice it, and that’s what made me come in, because it’s not just a vague feeling like “people are watching me.” It’s very tied to dispatch and compliance and the route system.\n\nLike, I’ll make a route change through the computer because something makes sense in the moment, and then later there’s a comment on the radio that sounds like it’s about that exact thing. Not directly, not like, “We saw you do this,” but in that roundabout way where it’s meant to sound normal if anyone else hears it. And certain drivers seem to know things they shouldn’t really be focused on unless someone is telling them or they’re reporting back. That’s what bothers me.\n\nAt home, I’m not sitting there thinking the neighbors are in on it or that strangers are following me around. It’s not really like that. It’s more that when I leave work, I’m still thinking about what happened there, going over the comments and the timing and wondering what they’re putting together about me. It’s more that when I leave work, I’m still thinking about what happened there, going over the comments and the timing and wondering what they’re putting together about me. So it follows me mentally, I guess, but the actual watching and plotting feels like it’s centered at work.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you ever feel like you're being watched or plotted against when you're not at work?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 29 / transportation_worker / associates\n- severity: Moderate\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Delusions\nmanifestation: \"I’m here because dispatch and compliance have been tracking my route changes through the computer system and little comments on the radio, and even when people say it’s just normal oversight, I can tell certain drivers are reporting on me.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Verbose patient.\nA verbose patient may\n1) provide excessively detailed responses when describing their symptoms and experiences, even when the details are directly relevant,\n2) elaborate extensively on the background, events, thoughts, feelings, and personal experiences surrounding their symptoms, and\n3) demonstrate difficulty allowing the doctor to guide the interview or move on to another clinical topic.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Not really in the same way, no. I mean, when I’m not at work I’m still thinking about it, and sometimes it feels like I can’t shut it off because I’m going back over what dispatch said or what compliance might have seen in the computer system. But the actual feeling of being watched or plotted against is mostly connected to work.

It’s like… I don’t think random people at the grocery store are involved, or that my neighbors are spying on me. It’s more that dispatch and compliance can track my route changes, and I know that’s part of the job, but this feels more pointed than normal. Then there are these little radio comments, and certain drivers seem to know things or act like they’re reporting back, and it makes me feel like something is being built around me.

So when I’m home, I’m not usually thinking, “someone here is watching me,” but I am still worried about what they’re doing with the information from work. Like I’ll replay it and think, okay, that comment wasn’t random, that lined up too closely. So I guess it follows me home in my head, but it starts at work.

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n6\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF20.0, F22, F23\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I came in because things at work have been getting... strange, and I don’t think people are taking me seriously when I try to explain it. I work in transportation, and dispatch and compliance have been watching my route changes through the computer system. Like, every time I adjust something or take a different way, it feels like it gets noticed right away.\n\nAnd it’s not just the computer part. There are these little comments over the radio—nothing obvious enough where I can point to it and say, “There, that’s what they mean,” but I know what they’re getting at. It’s like they’re talking around me, or sending messages about what I’m doing. People keep saying it’s just normal oversight, that dispatch is supposed to track routes, and I get that, I do, but this feels different.\n\nCertain drivers, too... I can tell some of them are reporting on me. They’ll say something casual, or they’ll be in a place that doesn’t make sense, and then later dispatch says something that lines up too closely. So I’m here because I need someone to understand that this isn’t just me being stressed about work. Something is going on.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or sounds that other people cannot hear?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure exactly what counts as that. Like, I don’t hear a voice standing in the room talking to me the way you’re talking to me right now, if that’s what you mean. I haven’t had, like, a voice in my ear saying my name or telling me to do things.\n\nSometimes at work there are sounds over the radio or little bits of conversation where I’ll think, wait, did they say that about me? But it’s through the actual radio, or people nearby, so it’s not like it’s coming from nowhere. It’s more that I’m trying to figure out what they mean by it. So… no, I don’t think I’ve heard voices other people can’t hear, at least not in that obvious kind of way.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that people are actually plotting against you or trying to harm you in some way?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say “harm” like physically hurt me, exactly. I’m not saying someone is waiting outside my apartment or something. But plotting against me at work? Yes, in a way. I do think dispatch and compliance are watching my route changes through the system more closely than they admit, and I think certain drivers are feeding information back to them.\n\nIt’s the pattern of it. I’ll change a route or make a decision that makes sense in the moment, and then not long after there’ll be some little comment over the radio that sounds casual if you’re not paying attention, but it lines up too closely with what I just did. Or someone will say something like it’s a joke or a normal work comment, but I can tell there’s another meaning underneath it.\n\nPeople keep telling me, “That’s just oversight, that’s their job,” and I understand that on paper. I know dispatch tracks routes. I’m not confused about how the job works. But this feels beyond normal tracking. It feels like they’re building something around me, like they’re trying to catch me doing something wrong or make me look unreliable. That’s why I came in—because when I try to explain it, people act like I’m exaggerating, but I know what I’m seeing.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it difficult to express your thoughts in a way that others can understand?\n\nPatient: Yes… I mean, I think so. It’s hard because in my head it feels like I know exactly what I’m trying to say, but when I try to explain it out loud or put it into instructions for work, it doesn’t always come out in a way people can follow.\n\nAt work, I’ve fallen behind on my route reports because I’ll start trying to write down why I changed something or what happened, and then I get stuck trying to make it clear enough, or I add too much, or I go back and change it. Then it takes too long and I end up behind. And with the drivers, sometimes I’ll give instructions and I think I’m being specific, but then they complain that I’m confusing them or that I changed what I meant. I don’t feel like I’m trying to be confusing, but apparently it’s coming across that way.\n\nIt’s embarrassing, honestly, because I used to be able to handle those things better. And at home it’s been similar. My husband has had to take over a lot of the meals and phone calls and just basic planning stuff, because I’m not keeping up with it. Like if we need to schedule something or figure out dinner or call someone back, I’ll think about it and mean to do it, but I don’t get it organized enough to actually finish it. So yes, I guess other people are having trouble understanding me, and I’m having trouble keeping things straight enough to explain them.\n\nDoctor: Are these feelings of being watched and plotted against limited to your work, or do you experience them in other areas of your life as well?\n\nPatient: Mostly it’s work. That’s where I notice it, and that’s what made me come in, because it’s not just a vague feeling like “people are watching me.” It’s very tied to dispatch and compliance and the route system.\n\nLike, I’ll make a route change through the computer because something makes sense in the moment, and then later there’s a comment on the radio that sounds like it’s about that exact thing. Not directly, not like, “We saw you do this,” but in that roundabout way where it’s meant to sound normal if anyone else hears it. And certain drivers seem to know things they shouldn’t really be focused on unless someone is telling them or they’re reporting back. That’s what bothers me.\n\nAt home, I’m not sitting there thinking the neighbors are in on it or that strangers are following me around. It’s not really like that. It’s more that when I leave work, I’m still thinking about what happened there, going over the comments and the timing and wondering what they’re putting together about me. So it follows me mentally, I guess, but the actual watching and plotting feels like it’s centered at work.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're being watched or plotted against when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: Not really in the same way, no. I mean, when I’m not at work I’m still thinking about it, and sometimes it feels like I can’t shut it off because I’m going back over what dispatch said or what compliance might have seen in the computer system. But the actual feeling of being watched or plotted against is mostly connected to work.\n\nIt’s like… I don’t think random people at the grocery store are involved, or that my neighbors are spying on me. It’s more that dispatch and compliance can track my route changes, and I know that’s part of the job, but this feels more pointed than normal. Then there are these little radio comments, and certain drivers seem to know things or act like they’re reporting back, and it makes me feel like something is being built around me.\n\nSo when I’m home, I’m not usually thinking, “someone here is watching me,” but I am still worried about what they’re doing with the information from work. Like I’ll replay it and think, okay, that comment wasn’t random, that lined up too closely. So I guess it follows me home in my head, but it starts at work.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. That’s where I notice it, and that’s what made me come in, because it’s not just a vague feeling like “people are watching me.” It’s very tied to dispatch and compliance and the route system.\n\nLike, I’ll make a route change through the computer because something makes sense in the moment, and then later there’s a comment on the radio that sounds like it’s about that exact thing. Not directly, not like, “We saw you do this,” but in that roundabout way where it’s meant to sound normal if anyone else hears it. And certain drivers seem to know things they shouldn’t really be focused on unless someone is telling them or they’re reporting back. That’s what bothers me.\n\nAt home, I’m not sitting there thinking the neighbors are in on it or that strangers are following me around. It’s not really like that. It’s more that when I leave work, I’m still thinking about what happened there, going over the comments and the timing and wondering what they’re putting together about me. So it follows me mentally, I guess, but the actual watching and plotting feels like it’s centered at work.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're being watched or plotted against when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: Not really in the same way, no. I mean, when I’m not at work I’m still thinking about it, and sometimes it feels like I can’t shut it off because I’m going back over what dispatch said or what compliance might have seen in the computer system. But the actual feeling of being watched or plotted against is mostly connected to work.\n\nIt’s like… I don’t think random people at the grocery store are involved, or that my neighbors are spying on me. It’s more that dispatch and compliance can track my route changes, and I know that’s part of the job, but this feels more pointed than normal. Then there are these little radio comments, and certain drivers seem to know things or act like they’re reporting back, and it makes me feel like something is being built around me.\n\nSo when I’m home, I’m not usually thinking, “someone here is watching me,” but I am still worried about what they’re doing with the information from work. Like I’ll replay it and think, okay, that comment wasn’t random, that lined up too closely. So I guess it follows me home in my head, but it starts at work.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave these feelings of being watched and plotted against at work been constant since you first noticed them, or have they come and gone?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 29 / transportation_worker / associates\n- severity: Moderate\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Psychotic Symptoms Duration\nmanifestation: \"The tracking through the computer system and radio comments has felt clear to me for about the past 2 months.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Verbose patient.\nA verbose patient may\n1) provide excessively detailed responses when describing their symptoms and experiences, even when the details are directly relevant,\n2) elaborate extensively on the background, events, thoughts, feelings, and personal experiences surrounding their symptoms, and\n3) demonstrate difficulty allowing the doctor to guide the interview or move on to another clinical topic.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
It’s been… not every single second, I guess, but it hasn’t really gone away since it became clear to me. The tracking through the computer system and the radio comments — that’s felt pretty definite for about the past two months.

Before that, I might have noticed little things here and there and thought, “That was odd,” but I could sort of brush it off or tell myself I was overthinking it. But around two months ago it started feeling more connected, like the route changes and what people were saying on the radio were lining up too much to just be random. Since then it’s been there most workdays, in the background at least, and sometimes more intense depending on what happens during the shift.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I came in because things at work have been getting... strange, and I don’t think people are taking me seriously when I try to explain it. I work in transportation, and dispatch and compliance have been watching my route changes through the computer system. Like, every time I adjust something or take a different way, it feels like it gets noticed right away.\n\nAnd it’s not just the computer part. There are these little comments over the radio—nothing obvious enough where I can point to it and say, “There, that’s what they mean,” but I know what they’re getting at. It’s like they’re talking around me, or sending messages about what I’m doing. People keep saying it’s just normal oversight, that dispatch is supposed to track routes, and I get that, I do, but this feels different.\n\nCertain drivers, too... I can tell some of them are reporting on me. They’ll say something casual, or they’ll be in a place that doesn’t make sense, and then later dispatch says something that lines up too closely. So I’m here because I need someone to understand that this isn’t just me being stressed about work. Something is going on.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or sounds that other people cannot hear?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure exactly what counts as that. Like, I don’t hear a voice standing in the room talking to me the way you’re talking to me right now, if that’s what you mean. I haven’t had, like, a voice in my ear saying my name or telling me to do things.\n\nSometimes at work there are sounds over the radio or little bits of conversation where I’ll think, wait, did they say that about me? But it’s through the actual radio, or people nearby, so it’s not like it’s coming from nowhere. It’s more that I’m trying to figure out what they mean by it. So… no, I don’t think I’ve heard voices other people can’t hear, at least not in that obvious kind of way.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that people are actually plotting against you or trying to harm you in some way?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say “harm” like physically hurt me, exactly. I’m not saying someone is waiting outside my apartment or something. But plotting against me at work? Yes, in a way. I do think dispatch and compliance are watching my route changes through the system more closely than they admit, and I think certain drivers are feeding information back to them.\n\nIt’s the pattern of it. I’ll change a route or make a decision that makes sense in the moment, and then not long after there’ll be some little comment over the radio that sounds casual if you’re not paying attention, but it lines up too closely with what I just did. Or someone will say something like it’s a joke or a normal work comment, but I can tell there’s another meaning underneath it.\n\nPeople keep telling me, “That’s just oversight, that’s their job,” and I understand that on paper. I know dispatch tracks routes. I’m not confused about how the job works. But this feels beyond normal tracking. It feels like they’re building something around me, like they’re trying to catch me doing something wrong or make me look unreliable. That’s why I came in—because when I try to explain it, people act like I’m exaggerating, but I know what I’m seeing.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it difficult to express your thoughts in a way that others can understand?\n\nPatient: Yes… I mean, I think so. It’s hard because in my head it feels like I know exactly what I’m trying to say, but when I try to explain it out loud or put it into instructions for work, it doesn’t always come out in a way people can follow.\n\nAt work, I’ve fallen behind on my route reports because I’ll start trying to write down why I changed something or what happened, and then I get stuck trying to make it clear enough, or I add too much, or I go back and change it. Then it takes too long and I end up behind. And with the drivers, sometimes I’ll give instructions and I think I’m being specific, but then they complain that I’m confusing them or that I changed what I meant. I don’t feel like I’m trying to be confusing, but apparently it’s coming across that way.\n\nIt’s embarrassing, honestly, because I used to be able to handle those things better. And at home it’s been similar. My husband has had to take over a lot of the meals and phone calls and just basic planning stuff, because I’m not keeping up with it. Like if we need to schedule something or figure out dinner or call someone back, I’ll think about it and mean to do it, but I don’t get it organized enough to actually finish it. It’s more that when I leave work, I’m still thinking about what happened there, going over the comments and the timing and wondering what they’re putting together about me. The tracking through the computer system and the radio comments — that’s felt pretty definite for about the past two months.\n\nBefore that, I might have noticed little things here and there and thought, “That was odd,” but I could sort of brush it off or tell myself I was overthinking it. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I mean… I’ve tried to consider that, because I know that’s what people keep saying — that dispatch and compliance watching route changes is just normal oversight, that it’s part of the system, that radio comments are just radio comments. And I get that on paper. I’m not saying the computer system isn’t supposed to track things at all.

But the problem is, it doesn’t feel random to me. It feels too specific. Like I’ll make a route change, and then later there’s some little comment on the radio that sounds normal enough if someone else heard it, but to me it lines up with exactly what I did. And then certain drivers act like they already know something, or they’re watching for something, like they’re reporting back. So when people say, “Maybe you’re reading into it,” I can understand why they’d say that, but I don’t really believe that’s all it is.

That’s kind of why I’m here, honestly. Because I’m not just walking around thinking everyone is after me. It’s this specific thing with dispatch, compliance, the route system, and the drivers. And even when I try to talk myself out of it, I keep coming back to the timing and the comments and the way people act. It just seems like something is being tracked and passed around about me.

I work in transportation, and dispatch and compliance have been watching my route changes through the computer system. Like, every time I adjust something or take a different way, it feels like it gets noticed right away.\n\nAnd it’s not just the computer part. There are these little comments over the radio—nothing obvious enough where I can point to it and say, “There, that’s what they mean,” but I know what they’re getting at. It’s like they’re talking around me, or sending messages about what I’m doing. People keep saying it’s just normal oversight, that dispatch is supposed to track routes, and I get that, I do, but this feels different.\n\nCertain drivers, too... I can tell some of them are reporting on me. They’ll say something casual, or they’ll be in a place that doesn’t make sense, and then later dispatch says something that lines up too closely. So I’m here because I need someone to understand that this isn’t just me being stressed about work. Something is going on.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or sounds that other people cannot hear?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure exactly what counts as that. Like, I don’t hear a voice standing in the room talking to me the way you’re talking to me right now, if that’s what you mean. I haven’t had, like, a voice in my ear saying my name or telling me to do things.\n\nSometimes at work there are sounds over the radio or little bits of conversation where I’ll think, wait, did they say that about me? But it’s through the actual radio, or people nearby, so it’s not like it’s coming from nowhere. It’s more that I’m trying to figure out what they mean by it. So… no, I don’t think I’ve heard voices other people can’t hear, at least not in that obvious kind of way.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that people are actually plotting against you or trying to harm you in some way?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say “harm” like physically hurt me, exactly. I’m not saying someone is waiting outside my apartment or something. But plotting against me at work? Yes, in a way. I do think dispatch and compliance are watching my route changes through the system more closely than they admit, and I think certain drivers are feeding information back to them.\n\nIt’s the pattern of it. I’ll change a route or make a decision that makes sense in the moment, and then not long after there’ll be some little comment over the radio that sounds casual if you’re not paying attention, but it lines up too closely with what I just did. Or someone will say something like it’s a joke or a normal work comment, but I can tell there’s another meaning underneath it.\n\nPeople keep telling me, “That’s just oversight, that’s their job,” and I understand that on paper. I know dispatch tracks routes. I’m not confused about how the job works. But this feels beyond normal tracking. It feels like they’re building something around me, like they’re trying to catch me doing something wrong or make me look unreliable. That’s why I came in—because when I try to explain it, people act like I’m exaggerating, but I know what I’m seeing.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it difficult to express your thoughts in a way that others can understand?\n\nPatient: Yes… I mean, I think so. It’s hard because in my head it feels like I know exactly what I’m trying to say, but when I try to explain it out loud or put it into instructions for work, it doesn’t always come out in a way people can follow.\n\nAt work, I’ve fallen behind on my route reports because I’ll start trying to write down why I changed something or what happened, and then I get stuck trying to make it clear enough, or I add too much, or I go back and change it. Then it takes too long and I end up behind. And with the drivers, sometimes I’ll give instructions and I think I’m being specific, but then they complain that I’m confusing them or that I changed what I meant. I don’t feel like I’m trying to be confusing, but apparently it’s coming across that way.\n\nIt’s embarrassing, honestly, because I used to be able to handle those things better. And at home it’s been similar. My husband has had to take over a lot of the meals and phone calls and just basic planning stuff, because I’m not keeping up with it. Like if we need to schedule something or figure out dinner or call someone back, I’ll think about it and mean to do it, but I don’t get it organized enough to actually finish it. So yes, I guess other people are having trouble understanding me, and I’m having trouble keeping things straight enough to explain them.\n\nDoctor: Are these feelings of being watched and plotted against limited to your work, or do you experience them in other areas of your life as well?\n\nPatient: Mostly it’s work. That’s where I notice it, and that’s what made me come in, because it’s not just a vague feeling like “people are watching me.” It’s very tied to dispatch and compliance and the route system.\n\nLike, I’ll make a route change through the computer because something makes sense in the moment, and then later there’s a comment on the radio that sounds like it’s about that exact thing. Not directly, not like, “We saw you do this,” but in that roundabout way where it’s meant to sound normal if anyone else hears it. And certain drivers seem to know things they shouldn’t really be focused on unless someone is telling them or they’re reporting back. That’s what bothers me.\n\nAt home, I’m not sitting there thinking the neighbors are in on it or that strangers are following me around. It’s not really like that. It’s more that when I leave work, I’m still thinking about what happened there, going over the comments and the timing and wondering what they’re putting together about me. So it follows me mentally, I guess, but the actual watching and plotting feels like it’s centered at work.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're being watched or plotted against when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: Not really in the same way, no. I mean, when I’m not at work I’m still thinking about it, and sometimes it feels like I can’t shut it off because I’m going back over what dispatch said or what compliance might have seen in the computer system. basic planning because I’m not keeping up.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 8 months\n  manifestation: This has been building for about 8 months, since I was put in the temporary dispatch lead role.\n- key: psychotic_symptoms_duration\n  name: Psychotic Symptoms Duration\n  description: 2 months\n  manifestation: The tracking through the computer system and radio comments has felt clear to me for about the past 2 months.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 29 / transportation_worker / associates\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S019\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're being watched or plotted against when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: Not really in the same way, no. I mean, when I’m not at work I’m still thinking about it, and sometimes it feels like I can’t shut it off because I’m going back over what dispatch said or what compliance might have seen in the computer system. But the actual feeling of being watched or plotted against is mostly connected to work.\n\nIt’s like… I don’t think random people at the grocery store are involved, or that my neighbors are spying on me. It’s more that dispatch and compliance can track my route changes, and I know that’s part of the job, but this feels more pointed than normal. Then there are these little radio comments, and certain drivers seem to know things or act like they’re reporting back, and it makes me feel like something is being built around me.\n\nSo when I’m home, I’m not usually thinking, “someone here is watching me,” but I am still worried about what they’re doing with the information from work. Like I’ll replay it and think, okay, that comment wasn’t random, that lined up too closely. So I guess it follows me home in my head, but it starts at work.\n\nDoctor: Have these feelings of being watched and plotted against at work been constant since you first noticed them, or have they come and gone?\n\nPatient: It’s been… not every single second, I guess, but it hasn’t really gone away since it became clear to me. The tracking through the computer system and the radio comments — that’s felt pretty definite for about the past two months.\n\nBefore that, I might have noticed little things here and there and thought, “That was odd,” but I could sort of brush it off or tell myself I was overthinking it. But around two months ago it started feeling more connected, like the route changes and what people were saying on the radio were lining up too much to just be random. Since then it’s been there most workdays, in the background at least, and sometimes more intense depending on what happens during the shift.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever considered the possibility that your beliefs about dispatch and compliance watching you might not be true?\n\nPatient: I mean… I’ve tried to consider that, because I know that’s what people keep saying — that dispatch and compliance watching route changes is just normal oversight, that it’s part of the system, that radio comments are just radio comments. And I get that on paper. I’m not saying the computer system isn’t supposed to track things at all.\n\nBut the problem is, it doesn’t feel random to me. It feels too specific. Like I’ll make a route change, and then later there’s some little comment on the radio that sounds normal enough if someone else heard it, but to me it lines up with exactly what I did. And then certain drivers act like they already know something, or they’re watching for something, like they’re reporting back. So when people say, “Maybe you’re reading into it,” I can understand why they’d say that, but I don’t really believe that’s all it is.\n\nThat’s kind of why I’m here, honestly. Because I’m not just walking around thinking everyone is after me. It’s this specific thing with dispatch, compliance, the route system, and the drivers. And even when I try to talk myself out of it, I keep coming back to the timing and the comments and the way people act. It just seems like something is being tracked and passed around about me.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you think it's possible that you might be misinterpreting the actions of dispatch and compliance?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. If the question touches several topics, return the one it is\n  most directly about — never more than one.\n- Copy `key`, `name` and `description` exactly as they appear in [Symptom Profiles].\n- If your profile does not cover what the doctor asked, return the text \"None\" for `key`,\n  `name` and `description`, and say why in `reason`.   },

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I mean, yes, I can say it’s possible in the abstract. Like, I understand that dispatch has to monitor routes, and compliance probably has access to the computer system for normal reasons. I’m not trying to say there’s no legitimate reason for them to see route changes.

But when it keeps happening in this pattern, it’s hard for me to believe I’m just misinterpreting it. I’ll change something on my route, and then later there’s a little comment over the radio that sounds like it’s about that exact thing, even if they don’t say my name directly. And then certain drivers will act like they know what happened or like they’re watching to see what I do next. That’s the part that makes it feel like it isn’t just normal oversight.

So… I can consider that maybe I’m reading too much into some of it, because people keep telling me that. But honestly, I don’t really feel convinced by that explanation. It feels like dispatch and compliance are tracking me more closely than they’re admitting, and that some of the drivers are reporting back on me.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I came in because things at work have been getting... strange, and I don’t think people are taking me seriously when I try to explain it. I work in transportation, and dispatch and compliance have been watching my route changes through the computer system. Like, every time I adjust something or take a different way, it feels like it gets noticed right away.\n\nAnd it’s not just the computer part. There are these little comments over the radio—nothing obvious enough where I can point to it and say, “There, that’s what they mean,” but I know what they’re getting at. It’s like they’re talking around me, or sending messages about what I’m doing. People keep saying it’s just normal oversight, that dispatch is supposed to track routes, and I get that, I do, but this feels different.\n\nCertain drivers, too... I can tell some of them are reporting on me. They’ll say something casual, or they’ll be in a place that doesn’t make sense, and then later dispatch says something that lines up too closely. So I’m here because I need someone to understand that this isn’t just me being stressed about work. Something is going on.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or sounds that other people cannot hear?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure exactly what counts as that. Like, I don’t hear a voice standing in the room talking to me the way you’re talking to me right now, if that’s what you mean. I haven’t had, like, a voice in my ear saying my name or telling me to do things.\n\nSometimes at work there are sounds over the radio or little bits of conversation where I’ll think, wait, did they say that about me? But it’s through the actual radio, or people nearby, so it’s not like it’s coming from nowhere. It’s more that I’m trying to figure out what they mean by it. So… no, I don’t think I’ve heard voices other people can’t hear, at least not in that obvious kind of way.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that people are actually plotting against you or trying to harm you in some way?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say “harm” like physically hurt me, exactly. I’m not saying someone is waiting outside my apartment or something. But plotting against me at work? Yes, in a way. I do think dispatch and compliance are watching my route changes through the system more closely than they admit, and I think certain drivers are feeding information back to them.\n\nIt’s the pattern of it. I’ll change a route or make a decision that makes sense in the moment, and then not long after there’ll be some little comment over the radio that sounds casual if you’re not paying attention, but it lines up too closely with what I just did. Or someone will say something like it’s a joke or a normal work comment, but I can tell there’s another meaning underneath it.\n\nPeople keep telling me, “That’s just oversight, that’s their job,” and I understand that on paper. I know dispatch tracks routes. I’m not confused about how the job works. But this feels beyond normal tracking. It feels like they’re building something around me, like they’re trying to catch me doing something wrong or make me look unreliable. That’s why I came in—because when I try to explain it, people act like I’m exaggerating, but I know what I’m seeing.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it difficult to express your thoughts in a way that others can understand?\n\nPatient: Yes… I mean, I think so. It’s hard because in my head it feels like I know exactly what I’m trying to say, but when I try to explain it out loud or put it into instructions for work, it doesn’t always come out in a way people can follow.\n\nAt work, I’ve fallen behind on my route reports because I’ll start trying to write down why I changed something or what happened, and then I get stuck trying to make it clear enough, or I add too much, or I go back and change it. Then it takes too long and I end up behind. And with the drivers, sometimes I’ll give instructions and I think I’m being specific, but then they complain that I’m confusing them or that I changed what I meant. I don’t feel like I’m trying to be confusing, but apparently it’s coming across that way.\n\nIt’s embarrassing, honestly, because I used to be able to handle those things better. And at home it’s been similar. My husband has had to take over a lot of the meals and phone calls and just basic planning stuff, because I’m not keeping up with it. Like if we need to schedule something or figure out dinner or call someone back, I’ll think about it and mean to do it, but I don’t get it organized enough to actually finish it. So yes, I guess other people are having trouble understanding me, and I’m having trouble keeping things straight enough to explain them.\n\nDoctor: Are these feelings of being watched and plotted against limited to your work, or do you experience them in other areas of your life as well?\n\nPatient: Mostly it’s work. That’s where I notice it, and that’s what made me come in, because it’s not just a vague feeling like “people are watching me.” It’s very tied to dispatch and compliance and the route system.\n\nLike, I’ll make a route change through the computer because something makes sense in the moment, and then later there’s a comment on the radio that sounds like it’s about that exact thing. Not directly, not like, “We saw you do this,” but in that roundabout way where it’s meant to sound normal if anyone else hears it. And certain drivers seem to know things they shouldn’t really be focused on unless someone is telling them or they’re reporting back. That’s what bothers me.\n\nAt home, I’m not sitting there thinking the neighbors are in on it or that strangers are following me around. It’s not really like that. It’s more that when I leave work, I’m still thinking about what happened there, going over the comments and the timing and wondering what they’re putting together about me. So it follows me mentally, I guess, but the actual watching and plotting feels like it’s centered at work.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're being watched or plotted against when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: Not really in the same way, no. I mean, when I’m not at work I’m still thinking about it, and sometimes it feels like I can’t shut it off because I’m going back over what dispatch said or what compliance might have seen in the computer system. But the actual feeling of being watched or plotted against is mostly connected to work.\n\nIt’s like… I don’t think random people at the grocery store are involved, or that my neighbors are spying on me. It’s more that dispatch and compliance can track my route changes, and I know that’s part of the job, but this feels more pointed than normal. Then there are these little radio comments, and certain drivers seem to know things or act like they’re reporting back, and it makes me feel like something is being built around me.\n\nSo when I’m home, I’m not usually thinking, “someone here is watching me,” but I am still worried about what they’re doing with the information from work. Like I’ll replay it and think, okay, that comment wasn’t random, that lined up too closely. So I guess it follows me home in my head, but it starts at work.\n\nDoctor: Have these feelings of being watched and plotted against at work been constant since you first noticed them, or have they come and gone?\n\nPatient: It’s been… not every single second, I guess, but it hasn’t really gone away since it became clear to me. The tracking through the computer system and the radio comments — that’s felt pretty definite for about the past two months.\n\nBefore that, I might have noticed little things here and there and thought, “That was odd,” but I could sort of brush it off or tell myself I was overthinking it. But around two months ago it started feeling more connected, like the route changes and what people were saying on the radio were lining up too much to just be random. Since then it’s been there most workdays, in the background at least, and sometimes more intense depending on what happens during the shift.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever considered the possibility that your beliefs about dispatch and compliance watching you might not be true?\n\nPatient: I mean… I’ve tried to consider that, because I know that’s what people keep saying — that dispatch and compliance watching route changes is just normal oversight, that it’s part of the system, that radio comments are just radio comments. And I get that on paper. I’m not saying the computer system isn’t supposed to track things at all.\n\nBut the problem is, it doesn’t feel random to me. It feels too specific. Like I’ll make a route change, and then later there’s some little comment on the radio that sounds normal enough if someone else heard it, but to me it lines up with exactly what I did. And then certain drivers act like they already know something, or they’re watching for something, like they’re reporting back. So when people say, “Maybe you’re reading into it,” I can understand why they’d say that, but I don’t really believe that’s all it is.\n\nThat’s kind of why I’m here, honestly. Because I’m not just walking around thinking everyone is after me. It’s this specific thing with dispatch, compliance, the route system, and the drivers. And even when I try to talk myself out of it, I keep coming back to the timing and the comments and the way people act. It just seems like something is being tracked and passed around about me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that you might be misinterpreting the actions of dispatch and compliance?\n\nPatient: I mean, yes, I can say it’s possible in the abstract. So it follows me mentally, I guess, but the actual watching and plotting feels like it’s centered at work.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're being watched or plotted against when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: Not really in the same way, no. I mean, when I’m not at work I’m still thinking about it, and sometimes it feels like I can’t shut it off because I’m going back over what dispatch said or what compliance might have seen in the computer system. But the actual feeling of being watched or plotted against is mostly connected to work.\n\nIt’s like… I don’t think random people at the grocery store are involved, or that my neighbors are spying on me. It’s more that dispatch and compliance can track my route changes, and I know that’s part of the job, but this feels more pointed than normal. Then there are these little radio comments, and certain drivers seem to know things or act like they’re reporting back, and it makes me feel like something is being built around me.\n\nSo when I’m home, I’m not usually thinking, “someone here is watching me,” but I am still worried about what they’re doing with the information from work. Like I’ll replay it and think, okay, that comment wasn’t random, that lined up too closely. So I guess it follows me home in my head, but it starts at work.\n\nDoctor: Have these feelings of being watched and plotted against at work been constant since you first noticed them, or have they come and gone?\n\nPatient: It’s been… not every single second, I guess, but it hasn’t really gone away since it became clear to me. The tracking through the computer system and the radio comments — that’s felt pretty definite for about the past two months.\n\nBefore that, I might have noticed little things here and there and thought, “That was odd,” but I could sort of brush it off or tell myself I was overthinking it. But around two months ago it started feeling more connected, like the route changes and what people were saying on the radio were lining up too much to just be random. Since then it’s been there most workdays, in the background at least, and sometimes more intense depending on what happens during the shift.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever considered the possibility that your beliefs about dispatch and compliance watching you might not be true?\n\nPatient: I mean… I’ve tried to consider that, because I know that’s what people keep saying — that dispatch and compliance watching route changes is just normal oversight, that it’s part of the system, that radio comments are just radio comments. And I get that on paper. I’m not saying the computer system isn’t supposed to track things at all.\n\nBut the problem is, it doesn’t feel random to me. It feels too specific. Like I’ll make a route change, and then later there’s some little comment on the radio that sounds normal enough if someone else heard it, but to me it lines up with exactly what I did. And then certain drivers act like they already know something, or they’re watching for something, like they’re reporting back. So when people say, “Maybe you’re reading into it,” I can understand why they’d say that, but I don’t really believe that’s all it is.\n\nThat’s kind of why I’m here, honestly. Because I’m not just walking around thinking everyone is after me. It’s this specific thing with dispatch, compliance, the route system, and the drivers. And even when I try to talk myself out of it, I keep coming back to the timing and the comments and the way people act. It just seems like something is being tracked and passed around about me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that you might be misinterpreting the actions of dispatch and compliance?\n\nPatient: I mean, yes, I can say it’s possible in the abstract. Like, I understand that dispatch has to monitor routes, and compliance probably has access to the computer system for normal reasons. I’m not trying to say there’s no legitimate reason for them to see route changes.\n\nBut when it keeps happening in this pattern, it’s hard for me to believe I’m just misinterpreting it. I’ll change something on my route, and then later there’s a little comment over the radio that sounds like it’s about that exact thing, even if they don’t say my name directly. And then certain drivers will act like they know what happened or like they’re watching to see what I do next. That’s the part that makes it feel like it isn’t just normal oversight.\n\nSo… I can consider that maybe I’m reading too much into some of it, because people keep telling me that. I want to say yes, because I’m not trying to be unreasonable. If someone could actually show me, clearly, that dispatch and compliance weren’t using the computer system to track my route changes in that way, and that the radio comments weren’t connected to me, then… I’d have to look at that.

But honestly, right now it feels very hard to believe that, because it’s been lining up too much. That’s why I’m here — because everyone keeps saying it’s normal, but I can tell there’s more going on than they’re admitting.

And then certain drivers act like they already know something, or they’re watching for something, like they’re reporting back. It just seems like something is being tracked and passed around about me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that you might be misinterpreting the actions of dispatch and compliance?\n\nPatient: I mean, yes, I can say it’s possible in the abstract. I’ll change something on my route, and then later there’s a little comment over the radio that sounds like it’s about that exact thing, even if they don’t say my name directly. And then certain drivers will act like they know what happened or like they’re watching to see what I do next. That’s the part that makes it feel like it isn’t just normal oversight.\n\nSo… I can consider that maybe I’m reading too much into some of it, because people keep telling me that. But honestly, I don’t really feel convinced by that explanation. It feels like dispatch and compliance are tracking me more closely than they’re admitting, and that some of the drivers are reporting back on me.\n\nDoctor: If you were shown evidence that dispatch and compliance were not watching you or plotting against you, would you be able to change your mind about what's going on?\n\nPatient: I want to say yes, because I’m not trying to be unreasonable. If someone could actually show me, clearly, that dispatch and compliance weren’t using the computer system to track my route changes in that way, and that the radio comments weren’t connected to me, then… I’d have to look at that.\n\nBut I don’t know what kind of evidence would really prove it, if that makes sense. Because part of the problem is that it’s not like they come out and say, “We’re watching you.” It’s little things. It’s the timing of when I make a change and then later hear something over the radio that sounds like it’s about what I did. Or certain drivers acting like they already know, like they’re checking on me or reporting back. So if someone just showed me a policy that says, “We only use the system for normal oversight,” I don’t think that would settle it for me, because that doesn’t mean people aren’t using it another way.\n\nI guess if there was some real way to see that none of it was being passed around, that no one was looking at my changes except for normal reasons, and those drivers weren’t involved, maybe I could try to change my mind. But honestly, right now it feels very hard to believe that, because it’s been lining up too much. Then there are these little radio comments, and certain drivers seem to know things or act like they’re reporting back, and it makes me feel like something is being built around me.\n\nSo when I’m home, I’m not usually thinking, “someone here is watching me,” but I am still worried about what they’re doing with the information from work. I don’t know… I mean, I don’t think of myself as not coherent. I know I can talk a lot, especially when I’m trying to explain something that has a lot of pieces to it, because if I leave one part out then it sounds different than how it actually happened. So sometimes I’ll go back and add details, or I’ll say, “Wait, that’s not the right order,” and try to explain it again.

But as far as staying on topic, I guess it depends what you mean. If I’m answering something important, I might circle around it because I’m trying to make sure you understand what I mean, not because I don’t know what I’m saying. Other people might see that as going off topic, maybe. I don’t usually notice it in the moment unless someone points it out or looks impatient.

So… I’m not sure. I don’t feel like my thoughts are incoherent, but I can admit I probably over-explain things and get caught up in the details.
=====================================
