What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I came in because things at work have been getting... strange, and I don’t think people are taking me seriously when I try to explain it. I work in transportation, and dispatch and compliance have been watching my route changes through the computer system. Like, every time I adjust something or take a different way, it feels like it gets noticed right away.\n\nAnd it’s not just the computer part. There are these little comments over the radio—nothing obvious enough where I can point to it and say, “There, that’s what they mean,” but I know what they’re getting at. It’s like they’re talking around me, or sending messages about what I’m doing. People keep saying it’s just normal oversight, that dispatch is supposed to track routes, and I get that, I do, but this feels different.\n\nCertain drivers, too... I can tell some of them are reporting on me. They’ll say something casual, or they’ll be in a place that doesn’t make sense, and then later dispatch says something that lines up too closely. So I’m here because I need someone to understand that this isn’t just me being stressed about work. Something is going on.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or sounds that other people cannot hear?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure exactly what counts as that. Like, I don’t hear a voice standing in the room talking to me the way you’re talking to me right now, if that’s what you mean. I haven’t had, like, a voice in my ear saying my name or telling me to do things.\n\nSometimes at work there are sounds over the radio or little bits of conversation where I’ll think, wait, did they say that about me? But it’s through the actual radio, or people nearby, so it’s not like it’s coming from nowhere. It’s more that I’m trying to figure out what they mean by it. So… no, I don’t think I’ve heard voices other people can’t hear, at least not in that obvious kind of way.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that people are actually plotting against you or trying to harm you in some way?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say “harm” like physically hurt me, exactly. I’m not saying someone is waiting outside my apartment or something. But plotting against me at work? Yes, in a way. I do think dispatch and compliance are watching my route changes through the system more closely than they admit, and I think certain drivers are feeding information back to them.\n\nIt’s the pattern of it. I’ll change a route or make a decision that makes sense in the moment, and then not long after there’ll be some little comment over the radio that sounds casual if you’re not paying attention, but it lines up too closely with what I just did. Or someone will say something like it’s a joke or a normal work comment, but I can tell there’s another meaning underneath it.\n\nPeople keep telling me, “That’s just oversight, that’s their job,” and I understand that on paper. I know dispatch tracks routes. I’m not confused about how the job works. But this feels beyond normal tracking. It feels like they’re building something around me, like they’re trying to catch me doing something wrong or make me look unreliable. That’s why I came in—because when I try to explain it, people act like I’m exaggerating, but I know what I’m seeing.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it difficult to express your thoughts in a way that others can understand?\n\nPatient: Yes… I mean, I think so. It’s hard because in my head it feels like I know exactly what I’m trying to say, but when I try to explain it out loud or put it into instructions for work, it doesn’t always come out in a way people can follow.\n\nAt work, I’ve fallen behind on my route reports because I’ll start trying to write down why I changed something or what happened, and then I get stuck trying to make it clear enough, or I add too much, or I go back and change it. Then it takes too long and I end up behind. And with the drivers, sometimes I’ll give instructions and I think I’m being specific, but then they complain that I’m confusing them or that I changed what I meant. I don’t feel like I’m trying to be confusing, but apparently it’s coming across that way.\n\nIt’s embarrassing, honestly, because I used to be able to handle those things better. And at home it’s been similar. My husband has had to take over a lot of the meals and phone calls and just basic planning stuff, because I’m not keeping up with it. Like if we need to schedule something or figure out dinner or call someone back, I’ll think about it and mean to do it, but I don’t get it organized enough to actually finish it. So yes, I guess other people are having trouble understanding me, and I’m having trouble keeping things straight enough to explain them.\n\nDoctor: Are these feelings of being watched and plotted against limited to your work, or do you experience them in other areas of your life as well?\n\nPatient: Mostly it’s work. That’s where I notice it, and that’s what made me come in, because it’s not just a vague feeling like “people are watching me.” It’s very tied to dispatch and compliance and the route system.\n\nLike, I’ll make a route change through the computer because something makes sense in the moment, and then later there’s a comment on the radio that sounds like it’s about that exact thing. Not directly, not like, “We saw you do this,” but in that roundabout way where it’s meant to sound normal if anyone else hears it. And certain drivers seem to know things they shouldn’t really be focused on unless someone is telling them or they’re reporting back. That’s what bothers me.\n\nAt home, I’m not sitting there thinking the neighbors are in on it or that strangers are following me around. It’s not really like that. It’s more that when I leave work, I’m still thinking about what happened there, going over the comments and the timing and wondering what they’re putting together about me. So it follows me mentally, I guess, but the actual watching and plotting feels like it’s centered at work.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're being watched or plotted against when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: Not really in the same way, no. I mean, when I’m not at work I’m still thinking about it, and sometimes it feels like I can’t shut it off because I’m going back over what dispatch said or what compliance might have seen in the computer system. But the actual feeling of being watched or plotted against is mostly connected to work.\n\nIt’s like… I don’t think random people at the grocery store are involved, or that my neighbors are spying on me. It’s more that dispatch and compliance can track my route changes, and I know that’s part of the job, but this feels more pointed than normal. Then there are these little radio comments, and certain drivers seem to know things or act like they’re reporting back, and it makes me feel like something is being built around me.\n\nSo when I’m home, I’m not usually thinking, “someone here is watching me,” but I am still worried about what they’re doing with the information from work. Like I’ll replay it and think, okay, that comment wasn’t random, that lined up too closely. So I guess it follows me home in my head, but it starts at work.\n\nDoctor: Have these feelings of being watched and plotted against at work been constant since you first noticed them, or have they come and gone?\n\nPatient: It’s been… not every single second, I guess, but it hasn’t really gone away since it became clear to me. The tracking through the computer system and the radio comments — that’s felt pretty definite for about the past two months.\n\nBefore that, I might have noticed little things here and there and thought, “That was odd,” but I could sort of brush it off or tell myself I was overthinking it. But around two months ago it started feeling more connected, like the route changes and what people were saying on the radio were lining up too much to just be random. Since then it’s been there most workdays, in the background at least, and sometimes more intense depending on what happens during the shift.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever considered the possibility that your beliefs about dispatch and compliance watching you might not be true?\n\nPatient: I mean… I’ve tried to consider that, because I know that’s what people keep saying — that dispatch and compliance watching route changes is just normal oversight, that it’s part of the system, that radio comments are just radio comments. And I get that on paper. I’m not saying the computer system isn’t supposed to track things at all.\n\nBut the problem is, it doesn’t feel random to me. It feels too specific. Like I’ll make a route change, and then later there’s some little comment on the radio that sounds normal enough if someone else heard it, but to me it lines up with exactly what I did. And then certain drivers act like they already know something, or they’re watching for something, like they’re reporting back. So when people say, “Maybe you’re reading into it,” I can understand why they’d say that, but I don’t really believe that’s all it is.\n\nThat’s kind of why I’m here, honestly. Because I’m not just walking around thinking everyone is after me. It’s this specific thing with dispatch, compliance, the route system, and the drivers. And even when I try to talk myself out of it, I keep coming back to the timing and the comments and the way people act. It just seems like something is being tracked and passed around about me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that you might be misinterpreting the actions of dispatch and compliance?\n\nPatient: I mean, yes, I can say it’s possible in the abstract. Like, I understand that dispatch has to monitor routes, and compliance probably has access to the computer system for normal reasons. I’m not trying to say there’s no legitimate reason for them to see route changes.\n\nBut when it keeps happening in this pattern, it’s hard for me to believe I’m just misinterpreting it. I’ll change something on my route, and then later there’s a little comment over the radio that sounds like it’s about that exact thing, even if they don’t say my name directly. And then certain drivers will act like they know what happened or like they’re watching to see what I do next. That’s the part that makes it feel like it isn’t just normal oversight.\n\nSo… I can consider that maybe I’m reading too much into some of it, because people keep telling me that. But honestly, I don’t really feel convinced by that explanation. It feels like dispatch and compliance are tracking me more closely than they’re admitting, and that some of the drivers are reporting back on me.\n\nDoctor: If you were shown evidence that dispatch and compliance were not watching you or plotting against you, would you be able to change your mind about what's going on?\n\nPatient: I want to say yes, because I’m not trying to be unreasonable. If someone could actually show me, clearly, that dispatch and compliance weren’t using the computer system to track my route changes in that way, and that the radio comments weren’t connected to me, then… I’d have to look at that.\n\nBut I don’t know what kind of evidence would really prove it, if that makes sense. Because part of the problem is that it’s not like they come out and say, “We’re watching you.” It’s little things. It’s the timing of when I make a change and then later hear something over the radio that sounds like it’s about what I did. Or certain drivers acting like they already know, like they’re checking on me or reporting back. So if someone just showed me a policy that says, “We only use the system for normal oversight,” I don’t think that would settle it for me, because that doesn’t mean people aren’t using it another way.\n\nI guess if there was some real way to see that none of it was being passed around, that no one was looking at my changes except for normal reasons, and those drivers weren’t involved, maybe I could try to change my mind. But honestly, right now it feels very hard to believe that, because it’s been lining up too much. That’s why I’m here — because everyone keeps saying it’s normal, but I can tell there’s more going on than they’re admitting.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself having trouble staying on topic or expressing your thoughts in a coherent manner?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I mean, I don’t think of myself as not coherent. I don’t feel like my thoughts are incoherent, but I can admit I probably over-explain things and get caught up in the details.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I’ve tried to think of other explanations, yeah. I mean, I know dispatch has to know where buses are, or where drivers are, and I know compliance has reasons to look at things in the system. So I’m not saying every single time someone checks a route it automatically means something bad.

And then certain drivers will act differently around me, like they already heard about it or like they’re waiting to see what I’m going to do next. That’s the part I can’t just explain away as normal work stuff.

I guess another explanation could be coincidence, or people just joking around, or me noticing things more because I’m already stressed about it. I can say that out loud. But it doesn’t feel like that’s what’s happening. It feels like dispatch and compliance are tracking my route changes through the computer system, and that some drivers are reporting back or being told to keep an eye on me.

So… I can think of other explanations, but I don’t really believe them as much, because the pattern has been too consistent.

I’ll change a route or make a decision that makes sense in the moment, and then not long after there’ll be some little comment over the radio that sounds casual if you’re not paying attention, but it lines up too closely with what I just did. Or someone will say something like it’s a joke or a normal work comment, but I can tell there’s another meaning underneath it.\n\nPeople keep telling me, “That’s just oversight, that’s their job,” and I understand that on paper. I know dispatch tracks routes. I’m not confused about how the job works. But this feels beyond normal tracking. It feels like they’re building something around me, like they’re trying to catch me doing something wrong or make me look unreliable. That’s why I came in—because when I try to explain it, people act like I’m exaggerating, but I know what I’m seeing.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it difficult to express your thoughts in a way that others can understand?\n\nPatient: Yes… I mean, I think so. It’s hard because in my head it feels like I know exactly what I’m trying to say, but when I try to explain it out loud or put it into instructions for work, it doesn’t always come out in a way people can follow.\n\nAt work, I’ve fallen behind on my route reports because I’ll start trying to write down why I changed something or what happened, and then I get stuck trying to make it clear enough, or I add too much, or I go back and change it. Then it takes too long and I end up behind. And with the drivers, sometimes I’ll give instructions and I think I’m being specific, but then they complain that I’m confusing them or that I changed what I meant. I don’t feel like I’m trying to be confusing, but apparently it’s coming across that way.\n\nIt’s embarrassing, honestly, because I used to be able to handle those things better. And at home it’s been similar. My husband has had to take over a lot of the meals and phone calls and just basic planning stuff, because I’m not keeping up with it. Like if we need to schedule something or figure out dinner or call someone back, I’ll think about it and mean to do it, but I don’t get it organized enough to actually finish it. So yes, I guess other people are having trouble understanding me, and I’m having trouble keeping things straight enough to explain them.\n\nDoctor: Are these feelings of being watched and plotted against limited to your work, or do you experience them in other areas of your life as well?\n\nPatient: Mostly it’s work. That’s where I notice it, and that’s what made me come in, because it’s not just a vague feeling like “people are watching me.” It’s very tied to dispatch and compliance and the route system.\n\nLike, I’ll make a route change through the computer because something makes sense in the moment, and then later there’s a comment on the radio that sounds like it’s about that exact thing. Not directly, not like, “We saw you do this,” but in that roundabout way where it’s meant to sound normal if anyone else hears it. And certain drivers seem to know things they shouldn’t really be focused on unless someone is telling them or they’re reporting back. That’s what bothers me.\n\nAt home, I’m not sitting there thinking the neighbors are in on it or that strangers are following me around. It’s not really like that. It’s more that when I leave work, I’m still thinking about what happened there, going over the comments and the timing and wondering what they’re putting together about me. So it follows me mentally, I guess, but the actual watching and plotting feels like it’s centered at work.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're being watched or plotted against when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: Not really in the same way, no. I mean, when I’m not at work I’m still thinking about it, and sometimes it feels like I can’t shut it off because I’m going back over what dispatch said or what compliance might have seen in the computer system. It’s more that dispatch and compliance can track my route changes, and I know that’s part of the job, but this feels more pointed than normal. Then there are these little radio comments, and certain drivers seem to know things or act like they’re reporting back, and it makes me feel like something is being built around me.\n\nSo when I’m home, I’m not usually thinking, “someone here is watching me,” but I am still worried about what they’re doing with the information from work. Like I’ll replay it and think, okay, that comment wasn’t random, that lined up too closely. The tracking through the computer system and the radio comments — that’s felt pretty definite for about the past two months.\n\nBefore that, I might have noticed little things here and there and thought, “That was odd,” but I could sort of brush it off or tell myself I was overthinking it. But around two months ago it started feeling more connected, like the route changes and what people were saying on the radio were lining up too much to just be random. I can tell some of them are reporting on me. They’ll say something casual, or they’ll be in a place that doesn’t make sense, and then later dispatch says something that lines up too closely. So I’m here because I need someone to understand that this isn’t just me being stressed about work. Something is going on.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or sounds that other people cannot hear?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure exactly what counts as that. Like, I don’t hear a voice standing in the room talking to me the way you’re talking to me right now, if that’s what you mean. I haven’t had, like, a voice in my ear saying my name or telling me to do things.\n\nSometimes at work there are sounds over the radio or little bits of conversation where I’ll think, wait, did they say that about me? But it’s through the actual radio, or people nearby, so it’s not like it’s coming from nowhere. It’s more that I’m trying to figure out what they mean by it. So… no, I don’t think I’ve heard voices other people can’t hear, at least not in that obvious kind of way.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that people are actually plotting against you or trying to harm you in some way?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say “harm” like physically hurt me, exactly. I’m not saying someone is waiting outside my apartment or something. But plotting against me at work? Yes, in a way. I do think dispatch and compliance are watching my route changes through the system more closely than they admit, and I think certain drivers are feeding information back to them.\n\nIt’s the pattern of it. I’ll change a route or make a decision that makes sense in the moment, and then not long after there’ll be some little comment over the radio that sounds casual if you’re not paying attention, but it lines up too closely with what I just did. Or someone will say something like it’s a joke or a normal work comment, but I can tell there’s another meaning underneath it.\n\nPeople keep telling me, “That’s just oversight, that’s their job,” and I understand that on paper. I know dispatch tracks routes. I’m not confused about how the job works. But this feels beyond normal tracking. It feels like they’re building something around me, like they’re trying to catch me doing something wrong or make me look unreliable. That’s why I came in—because when I try to explain it, people act like I’m exaggerating, but I know what I’m seeing.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it difficult to express your thoughts in a way that others can understand?\n\nPatient: Yes… I mean, I think so. It’s hard because in my head it feels like I know exactly what I’m trying to say, but when I try to explain it out loud or put it into instructions for work, it doesn’t always come out in a way people can follow.\n\nAt work, I’ve fallen behind on my route reports because I’ll start trying to write down why I changed something or what happened, and then I get stuck trying to make it clear enough, or I add too much, or I go back and change it. Then it takes too long and I end up behind. And with the drivers, sometimes I’ll give instructions and I think I’m being specific, but then they complain that I’m confusing them or that I changed what I meant. I don’t feel like I’m trying to be confusing, but apparently it’s coming across that way.\n\nIt’s embarrassing, honestly, because I used to be able to handle those things better. And at home it’s been similar. My husband has had to take over a lot of the meals and phone calls and just basic planning stuff, because I’m not keeping up with it. Like if we need to schedule something or figure out dinner or call someone back, I’ll think about it and mean to do it, but I don’t get it organized enough to actually finish it. So yes, I guess other people are having trouble understanding me, and I’m having trouble keeping things straight enough to explain them.\n\nDoctor: Are these feelings of being watched and plotted against limited to your work, or do you experience them in other areas of your life as well?\n\nPatient: Mostly it’s work. That’s where I notice it, and that’s what made me come in, because it’s not just a vague feeling like “people are watching me.” It’s very tied to dispatch and compliance and the route system.\n\nLike, I’ll make a route change through the computer because something makes sense in the moment, and then later there’s a comment on the radio that sounds like it’s about that exact thing. Not directly, not like, “We saw you do this,” but in that roundabout way where it’s meant to sound normal if anyone else hears it. And certain drivers seem to know things they shouldn’t really be focused on unless someone is telling them or they’re reporting back. That’s what bothers me.\n\nAt home, I’m not sitting there thinking the neighbors are in on it or that strangers are following me around. It’s not really like that. It’s more that when I leave work, I’m still thinking about what happened there, going over the comments and the timing and wondering what they’re putting together about me. So it follows me mentally, I guess, but the actual watching and plotting feels like it’s centered at work.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're being watched or plotted against when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: Not really in the same way, no. I mean, when I’m not at work I’m still thinking about it, and sometimes it feels like I can’t shut it off because I’m going back over what dispatch said or what compliance might have seen in the computer system. But the actual feeling of being watched or plotted against is mostly connected to work.\n\nIt’s like… I don’t think random people at the grocery store are involved, or that my neighbors are spying on me. It’s more that dispatch and compliance can track my route changes, and I know that’s part of the job, but this feels more pointed than normal. Then there are these little radio comments, and certain drivers seem to know things or act like they’re reporting back, and it makes me feel like something is being built around me.\n\nSo when I’m home, I’m not usually thinking, “someone here is watching me,” but I am still worried about what they’re doing with the information from work. Like I’ll replay it and think, okay, that comment wasn’t random, that lined up too closely. So I guess it follows me home in my head, but it starts at work.\n\nDoctor: Have these feelings of being watched and plotted against at work been constant since you first noticed them, or have they come and gone?\n\nPatient: It’s been… not every single second, I guess, but it hasn’t really gone away since it became clear to me. The tracking through the computer system and the radio comments — that’s felt pretty definite for about the past two months.\n\nBefore that, I might have noticed little things here and there and thought, “That was odd,” but I could sort of brush it off or tell myself I was overthinking it. But around two months ago it started feeling more connected, like the route changes and what people were saying on the radio were lining up too much to just be random. Since then it’s been there most workdays, in the background at least, and sometimes more intense depending on what happens during the shift.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever considered the possibility that your beliefs about dispatch and compliance watching you might not be true?\n\nPatient: I mean… I’ve tried to consider that, because I know that’s what people keep saying — that dispatch and compliance watching route changes is just normal oversight, that it’s part of the system, that radio comments are just radio comments. And I get that on paper. I’m not saying the computer system isn’t supposed to track things at all.\n\nBut the problem is, it doesn’t feel random to me. It feels too specific. Like I’ll make a route change, and then later there’s some little comment on the radio that sounds normal enough if someone else heard it, but to me it lines up with exactly what I did. And then certain drivers act like they already know something, or they’re watching for something, like they’re reporting back. So when people say, “Maybe you’re reading into it,” I can understand why they’d say that, but I don’t really believe that’s all it is.\n\nThat’s kind of why I’m here, honestly. Because I’m not just walking around thinking everyone is after me. It’s this specific thing with dispatch, compliance, the route system, and the drivers. And even when I try to talk myself out of it, I keep coming back to the timing and the comments and the way people act. It just seems like something is being tracked and passed around about me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that you might be misinterpreting the actions of dispatch and compliance?\n\nPatient: I mean, yes, I can say it’s possible in the abstract. Like, I understand that dispatch has to monitor routes, and compliance probably has access to the computer system for normal reasons. I’m not trying to say there’s no legitimate reason for them to see route changes.\n\nBut when it keeps happening in this pattern, it’s hard for me to believe I’m just misinterpreting it. I’ll change something on my route, and then later there’s a little comment over the radio that sounds like it’s about that exact thing, even if they don’t say my name directly. And then certain drivers will act like they know what happened or like they’re watching to see what I do next. That’s the part that makes it feel like it isn’t just normal oversight.\n\nSo… I can consider that maybe I’m reading too much into some of it, because people keep telling me that. But honestly, I don’t really feel convinced by that explanation. It feels like dispatch and compliance are tracking me more closely than they’re admitting, and that some of the drivers are reporting back on me.\n\nDoctor: If you were shown evidence that dispatch and compliance were not watching you or plotting against you, would you be able to change your mind about what's going on?\n\nPatient: I want to say yes, because I’m not trying to be unreasonable. If someone could actually show me, clearly, that dispatch and compliance weren’t using the computer system to track my route changes in that way, and that the radio comments weren’t connected to me, then… I’d have to look at that.\n\nBut I don’t know what kind of evidence would really prove it, if that makes sense. Because part of the problem is that it’s not like they come out and say, “We’re watching you.” It’s little things. It’s the timing of when I make a change and then later hear something over the radio that sounds like it’s about what I did. Or certain drivers acting like they already know, like they’re checking on me or reporting back. So if someone just showed me a policy that says, “We only use the system for normal oversight,” I don’t think that would settle it for me, because that doesn’t mean people aren’t using it another way.\n\nI guess if there was some real way to see that none of it was being passed around, that no one was looking at my changes except for normal reasons, and those drivers weren’t involved, maybe I could try to change my mind. But honestly, right now it feels very hard to believe that, because it’s been lining up too much. That’s why I’m here — because everyone keeps saying it’s normal, but I can tell there’s more going on than they’re admitting.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself having trouble staying on topic or expressing your thoughts in a coherent manner?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I mean, I don’t think of myself as not coherent. I know I can talk a lot, especially when I’m trying to explain something that has a lot of pieces to it, because if I leave one part out then it sounds different than how it actually happened. So sometimes I’ll go back and add details, or I’ll say, “Wait, that’s not the right order,” and try to explain it again.\n\nBut as far as staying on topic, I guess it depends what you mean. If I’m answering something important, I might circle around it because I’m trying to make sure you understand what I mean, not because I don’t know what I’m saying. Other people might see that as going off topic, maybe. I don’t usually notice it in the moment unless someone points it out or looks impatient.\n\nSo… I’m not sure. I don’t feel like my thoughts are incoherent, but I can admit I probably over-explain things and get caught up in the details.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any other explanation for the events you've described at work, other than dispatch and compliance watching you?\n\nPatient: I’ve tried to think of other explanations, yeah. I mean, I know dispatch has to know where buses are, or where drivers are, and I know compliance has reasons to look at things in the system. So I’m not saying every single time someone checks a route it automatically means something bad.\n\nBut the problem is the way it lines up. Like, if I make a route change or do something a little different, then later there’ll be a comment over the radio that isn’t technically naming me, but it fits too closely. And then certain drivers will act differently around me, like they already heard about it or like they’re waiting to see what I’m going to do next. That’s the part I can’t just explain away as normal work stuff.\n\nI guess another explanation could be coincidence, or people just joking around, or me noticing things more because I’m already stressed about it. I can say that out loud. But it doesn’t feel like that’s what’s happening. It feels like dispatch and compliance are tracking my route changes through the computer system, and that some drivers are reporting back or being told to keep an eye on me.\n\nSo… I can think of other explanations, but I don’t really believe them as much, because the pattern has been too consistent.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I would want to believe them. I mean, if it was someone I really trusted, like family, I wouldn’t think they were trying to hurt me or lie to me on purpose. So I’d listen. I’d probably ask them what exactly they looked at, though, because just saying “I didn’t find anything” wouldn’t completely settle it for me.

It’s hard because the whole reason I’m here is that this has been happening through things that aren’t obvious. Dispatch and compliance aren’t going to put it in writing like, “Yes, we’re tracking her route changes and talking about it over the radio.” It’s more like I’ll make a change in the system, and then later there’s some little radio comment that fits too well, or certain drivers act like they already know what I did, like they’re reporting back or watching to see if I do it again.

So if my family member said they investigated, I’d probably feel torn. Part of me would think, “Okay, maybe I should calm down and consider that.” But another part of me would wonder if they just weren’t shown the real thing, or if dispatch and compliance made it look normal because that’s what they’re supposed to do. Normal oversight is one thing, but what I’m talking about feels different.

So… I don’t know if I’d fully believe it right away. I’d take it seriously because I trust them, but I don’t think it would erase what I’ve noticed. Not unless they could really explain the timing, the radio comments, and why certain drivers seem like they’re involved.

    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n13\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF20.0, F22, F23\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I came in because things at work have been getting... strange, and I don’t think people are taking me seriously when I try to explain it. I work in transportation, and dispatch and compliance have been watching my route changes through the computer system. Like, every time I adjust something or take a different way, it feels like it gets noticed right away.\n\nAnd it’s not just the computer part. There are these little comments over the radio—nothing obvious enough where I can point to it and say, “There, that’s what they mean,” but I know what they’re getting at. It’s like they’re talking around me, or sending messages about what I’m doing. People keep saying it’s just normal oversight, that dispatch is supposed to track routes, and I get that, I do, but this feels different.\n\nCertain drivers, too... I can tell some of them are reporting on me. They’ll say something casual, or they’ll be in a place that doesn’t make sense, and then later dispatch says something that lines up too closely. So I’m here because I need someone to understand that this isn’t just me being stressed about work. Something is going on.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or sounds that other people cannot hear?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure exactly what counts as that. Like, I don’t hear a voice standing in the room talking to me the way you’re talking to me right now, if that’s what you mean. I haven’t had, like, a voice in my ear saying my name or telling me to do things.\n\nSometimes at work there are sounds over the radio or little bits of conversation where I’ll think, wait, did they say that about me? But it’s through the actual radio, or people nearby, so it’s not like it’s coming from nowhere. It’s more that I’m trying to figure out what they mean by it. So… no, I don’t think I’ve heard voices other people can’t hear, at least not in that obvious kind of way.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that people are actually plotting against you or trying to harm you in some way?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say “harm” like physically hurt me, exactly. I’m not saying someone is waiting outside my apartment or something. But plotting against me at work? Yes, in a way. I do think dispatch and compliance are watching my route changes through the system more closely than they admit, and I think certain drivers are feeding information back to them.\n\nIt’s the pattern of it. I’ll change a route or make a decision that makes sense in the moment, and then not long after there’ll be some little comment over the radio that sounds casual if you’re not paying attention, but it lines up too closely with what I just did. Or someone will say something like it’s a joke or a normal work comment, but I can tell there’s another meaning underneath it.\n\nPeople keep telling me, “That’s just oversight, that’s their job,” and I understand that on paper. I know dispatch tracks routes. I’m not confused about how the job works. But this feels beyond normal tracking. It feels like they’re building something around me, like they’re trying to catch me doing something wrong or make me look unreliable. That’s why I came in—because when I try to explain it, people act like I’m exaggerating, but I know what I’m seeing.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it difficult to express your thoughts in a way that others can understand?\n\nPatient: Yes… I mean, I think so. It’s hard because in my head it feels like I know exactly what I’m trying to say, but when I try to explain it out loud or put it into instructions for work, it doesn’t always come out in a way people can follow.\n\nAt work, I’ve fallen behind on my route reports because I’ll start trying to write down why I changed something or what happened, and then I get stuck trying to make it clear enough, or I add too much, or I go back and change it. Then it takes too long and I end up behind. And with the drivers, sometimes I’ll give instructions and I think I’m being specific, but then they complain that I’m confusing them or that I changed what I meant. I don’t feel like I’m trying to be confusing, but apparently it’s coming across that way.\n\nIt’s embarrassing, honestly, because I used to be able to handle those things better. And at home it’s been similar. My husband has had to take over a lot of the meals and phone calls and just basic planning stuff, because I’m not keeping up with it. Like if we need to schedule something or figure out dinner or call someone back, I’ll think about it and mean to do it, but I don’t get it organized enough to actually finish it. So yes, I guess other people are having trouble understanding me, and I’m having trouble keeping things straight enough to explain them.\n\nDoctor: Are these feelings of being watched and plotted against limited to your work, or do you experience them in other areas of your life as well?\n\nPatient: Mostly it’s work. That’s where I notice it, and that’s what made me come in, because it’s not just a vague feeling like “people are watching me.” It’s very tied to dispatch and compliance and the route system.\n\nLike, I’ll make a route change through the computer because something makes sense in the moment, and then later there’s a comment on the radio that sounds like it’s about that exact thing. Not directly, not like, “We saw you do this,” but in that roundabout way where it’s meant to sound normal if anyone else hears it. And certain drivers seem to know things they shouldn’t really be focused on unless someone is telling them or they’re reporting back. That’s what bothers me.\n\nAt home, I’m not sitting there thinking the neighbors are in on it or that strangers are following me around. It’s not really like that. It’s more that when I leave work, I’m still thinking about what happened there, going over the comments and the timing and wondering what they’re putting together about me. So it follows me mentally, I guess, but the actual watching and plotting feels like it’s centered at work.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're being watched or plotted against when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: Not really in the same way, no. I mean, when I’m not at work I’m still thinking about it, and sometimes it feels like I can’t shut it off because I’m going back over what dispatch said or what compliance might have seen in the computer system. But the actual feeling of being watched or plotted against is mostly connected to work.\n\nIt’s like… I don’t think random people at the grocery store are involved, or that my neighbors are spying on me. It’s more that dispatch and compliance can track my route changes, and I know that’s part of the job, but this feels more pointed than normal. Then there are these little radio comments, and certain drivers seem to know things or act like they’re reporting back, and it makes me feel like something is being built around me.\n\nSo when I’m home, I’m not usually thinking, “someone here is watching me,” but I am still worried about what they’re doing with the information from work. Like I’ll replay it and think, okay, that comment wasn’t random, that lined up too closely. So I guess it follows me home in my head, but it starts at work.\n\nDoctor: Have these feelings of being watched and plotted against at work been constant since you first noticed them, or have they come and gone?\n\nPatient: It’s been… not every single second, I guess, but it hasn’t really gone away since it became clear to me. The tracking through the computer system and the radio comments — that’s felt pretty definite for about the past two months.\n\nBefore that, I might have noticed little things here and there and thought, “That was odd,” but I could sort of brush it off or tell myself I was overthinking it. But around two months ago it started feeling more connected, like the route changes and what people were saying on the radio were lining up too much to just be random. Since then it’s been there most workdays, in the background at least, and sometimes more intense depending on what happens during the shift.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever considered the possibility that your beliefs about dispatch and compliance watching you might not be true?\n\nPatient: I mean… I’ve tried to consider that, because I know that’s what people keep saying — that dispatch and compliance watching route changes is just normal oversight, that it’s part of the system, that radio comments are just radio comments. And I get that on paper. I’m not saying the computer system isn’t supposed to track things at all.\n\nBut the problem is, it doesn’t feel random to me. It feels too specific. Like I’ll make a route change, and then later there’s some little comment on the radio that sounds normal enough if someone else heard it, but to me it lines up with exactly what I did. And then certain drivers act like they already know something, or they’re watching for something, like they’re reporting back. So when people say, “Maybe you’re reading into it,” I can understand why they’d say that, but I don’t really believe that’s all it is.\n\nThat’s kind of why I’m here, honestly. Because I’m not just walking around thinking everyone is after me. It’s this specific thing with dispatch, compliance, the route system, and the drivers. And even when I try to talk myself out of it, I keep coming back to the timing and the comments and the way people act. It just seems like something is being tracked and passed around about me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that you might be misinterpreting the actions of dispatch and compliance?\n\nPatient: I mean, yes, I can say it’s possible in the abstract. Like, I understand that dispatch has to monitor routes, and compliance probably has access to the computer system for normal reasons. I’m not trying to say there’s no legitimate reason for them to see route changes.\n\nBut when it keeps happening in this pattern, it’s hard for me to believe I’m just misinterpreting it. I’ll change something on my route, and then later there’s a little comment over the radio that sounds like it’s about that exact thing, even if they don’t say my name directly. And then certain drivers will act like they know what happened or like they’re watching to see what I do next. That’s the part that makes it feel like it isn’t just normal oversight.\n\nSo… I can consider that maybe I’m reading too much into some of it, because people keep telling me that. But honestly, I don’t really feel convinced by that explanation. It feels like dispatch and compliance are tracking me more closely than they’re admitting, and that some of the drivers are reporting back on me.\n\nDoctor: If you were shown evidence that dispatch and compliance were not watching you or plotting against you, would you be able to change your mind about what's going on?\n\nPatient: I want to say yes, because I’m not trying to be unreasonable. If someone could actually show me, clearly, that dispatch and compliance weren’t using the computer system to track my route changes in that way, and that the radio comments weren’t connected to me, then… I’d have to look at that.\n\nBut I don’t know what kind of evidence would really prove it, if that makes sense. Because part of the problem is that it’s not like they come out and say, “We’re watching you.” It’s little things. It’s the timing of when I make a change and then later hear something over the radio that sounds like it’s about what I did. Or certain drivers acting like they already know, like they’re checking on me or reporting back. So if someone just showed me a policy that says, “We only use the system for normal oversight,” I don’t think that would settle it for me, because that doesn’t mean people aren’t using it another way.\n\nI guess if there was some real way to see that none of it was being passed around, that no one was looking at my changes except for normal reasons, and those drivers weren’t involved, maybe I could try to change my mind. But honestly, right now it feels very hard to believe that, because it’s been lining up too much. That’s why I’m here — because everyone keeps saying it’s normal, but I can tell there’s more going on than they’re admitting.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself having trouble staying on topic or expressing your thoughts in a coherent manner?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I mean, I don’t think of myself as not coherent. I know I can talk a lot, especially when I’m trying to explain something that has a lot of pieces to it, because if I leave one part out then it sounds different than how it actually happened. So sometimes I’ll go back and add details, or I’ll say, “Wait, that’s not the right order,” and try to explain it again.\n\nBut as far as staying on topic, I guess it depends what you mean. If I’m answering something important, I might circle around it because I’m trying to make sure you understand what I mean, not because I don’t know what I’m saying. Other people might see that as going off topic, maybe. I don’t usually notice it in the moment unless someone points it out or looks impatient.\n\nSo… I’m not sure. I don’t feel like my thoughts are incoherent, but I can admit I probably over-explain things and get caught up in the details.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any other explanation for the events you've described at work, other than dispatch and compliance watching you?\n\nPatient: I’ve tried to think of other explanations, yeah. I mean, I know dispatch has to know where buses are, or where drivers are, and I know compliance has reasons to look at things in the system. So I’m not saying every single time someone checks a route it automatically means something bad.\n\nBut the problem is the way it lines up. Like, if I make a route change or do something a little different, then later there’ll be a comment over the radio that isn’t technically naming me, but it fits too closely. And then certain drivers will act differently around me, like they already heard about it or like they’re waiting to see what I’m going to do next. That’s the part I can’t just explain away as normal work stuff.\n\nI guess another explanation could be coincidence, or people just joking around, or me noticing things more because I’m already stressed about it. I can say that out loud. But it doesn’t feel like that’s what’s happening. It feels like dispatch and compliance are tracking my route changes through the computer system, and that some drivers are reporting back or being told to keep an eye on me.\n\nSo… I can think of other explanations, but I don’t really believe them as much, because the pattern has been too consistent.\n\nDoctor: If someone you trusted completely, like a close family member, told you that they had investigated and found no evidence of dispatch and compliance watching you, would you believe them?\n\nPatient: I would want to believe them. I mean, if it was someone I really trusted, like family, I wouldn’t think they were trying to hurt me or lie to me on purpose. So I’d listen. I’d probably ask them what exactly they looked at, though, because just saying “I didn’t find anything” wouldn’t completely settle it for me.\n\nIt’s hard because the whole reason I’m here is that this has been happening through things that aren’t obvious. Dispatch and compliance aren’t going to put it in writing like, “Yes, we’re tracking her route changes and talking about it over the radio.” It’s more like I’ll make a change in the system, and then later there’s some little radio comment that fits too well, or certain drivers act like they already know what I did, like they’re reporting back or watching to see if I do it again.\n\nSo if my family member said they investigated, I’d probably feel torn. Part of me would think, “Okay, maybe I should calm down and consider that.” But another part of me would wonder if they just weren’t shown the real thing, or if dispatch and compliance made it look normal because that’s what they’re supposed to do. Normal oversight is one thing, but what I’m talking about feels different.\n\nSo… I don’t know if I’d fully believe it right away. I’d take it seriously because I trust them, but I don’t think it would erase what I’ve noticed. Not unless they could really explain the timing, the radio comments, and why certain drivers seem like they’re involved.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }
]

I don’t want to say “no amount,” because that sounds like I’m being unreasonable or like I’ve made up my mind forever and nothing could ever change it. I don’t think of it that way. So if the evidence was just, “There’s no official report” or “Compliance says they aren’t watching you,” I don’t think that would be enough. Because of course they wouldn’t put it that way. But if there was some way to really show that those comments weren’t connected, and the drivers weren’t involved, and the computer tracking was only routine and not being used to single me out… then maybe. I’d want that to be true, honestly. I don’t like feeling like this. But right now, from what I’ve seen, it still feels too connected to just ignore.

I do think dispatch and compliance are watching my route changes through the system more closely than they admit, and I think certain drivers are feeding information back to them.\n\nIt’s the pattern of it. I’ll change a route or make a decision that makes sense in the moment, and then not long after there’ll be some little comment over the radio that sounds casual if you’re not paying attention, but it lines up too closely with what I just did. Or someone will say something like it’s a joke or a normal work comment, but I can tell there’s another meaning underneath it.\n\nPeople keep telling me, “That’s just oversight, that’s their job,” and I understand that on paper. I know dispatch tracks routes. I’m not confused about how the job works. But this feels beyond normal tracking. It feels like they’re building something around me, like they’re trying to catch me doing something wrong or make me look unreliable. That’s why I came in—because when I try to explain it, people act like I’m exaggerating, but I know what I’m seeing.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it difficult to express your thoughts in a way that others can understand?\n\nPatient: Yes… I mean, I think so. It’s hard because in my head it feels like I know exactly what I’m trying to say, but when I try to explain it out loud or put it into instructions for work, it doesn’t always come out in a way people can follow.\n\nAt work, I’ve fallen behind on my route reports because I’ll start trying to write down why I changed something or what happened, and then I get stuck trying to make it clear enough, or I add too much, or I go back and change it. Then it takes too long and I end up behind. And with the drivers, sometimes I’ll give instructions and I think I’m being specific, but then they complain that I’m confusing them or that I changed what I meant. I don’t feel like I’m trying to be confusing, but apparently it’s coming across that way.\n\nIt’s embarrassing, honestly, because I used to be able to handle those things better. And at home it’s been similar. My husband has had to take over a lot of the meals and phone calls and just basic planning stuff, because I’m not keeping up with it. Like if we need to schedule something or figure out dinner or call someone back, I’ll think about it and mean to do it, but I don’t get it organized enough to actually finish it. So yes, I guess other people are having trouble understanding me, and I’m having trouble keeping things straight enough to explain them.\n\nDoctor: Are these feelings of being watched and plotted against limited to your work, or do you experience them in other areas of your life as well?\n\nPatient: Mostly it’s work. That’s where I notice it, and that’s what made me come in, because it’s not just a vague feeling like “people are watching me.” It’s very tied to dispatch and compliance and the route system.\n\nLike, I’ll make a route change through the computer because something makes sense in the moment, and then later there’s a comment on the radio that sounds like it’s about that exact thing. Not directly, not like, “We saw you do this,” but in that roundabout way where it’s meant to sound normal if anyone else hears it. And certain drivers seem to know things they shouldn’t really be focused on unless someone is telling them or they’re reporting back. That’s what bothers me.\n\nAt home, I’m not sitting there thinking the neighbors are in on it or that strangers are following me around. It’s not really like that. It’s more that when I leave work, I’m still thinking about what happened there, going over the comments and the timing and wondering what they’re putting together about me. So it follows me mentally, I guess, but the actual watching and plotting feels like it’s centered at work.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're being watched or plotted against when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: Not really in the same way, no. I mean, when I’m not at work I’m still thinking about it, and sometimes it feels like I can’t shut it off because I’m going back over what dispatch said or what compliance might have seen in the computer system. But the actual feeling of being watched or plotted against is mostly connected to work.\n\nIt’s like… I don’t think random people at the grocery store are involved, or that my neighbors are spying on me. It’s more that dispatch and compliance can track my route changes, and I know that’s part of the job, but this feels more pointed than normal. Then there are these little radio comments, and certain drivers seem to know things or act like they’re reporting back, and it makes me feel like something is being built around me.\n\nSo when I’m home, I’m not usually thinking, “someone here is watching me,” but I am still worried about what they’re doing with the information from work. Like I’ll replay it and think, okay, that comment wasn’t random, that lined up too closely. So I guess it follows me home in my head, but it starts at work.\n\nDoctor: Have these feelings of being watched and plotted against at work been constant since you first noticed them, or have they come and gone?\n\nPatient: It’s been… not every single second, I guess, but it hasn’t really gone away since it became clear to me. The tracking through the computer system and the radio comments — that’s felt pretty definite for about the past two months.\n\nBefore that, I might have noticed little things here and there and thought, “That was odd,” but I could sort of brush it off or tell myself I was overthinking it. But around two months ago it started feeling more connected, like the route changes and what people were saying on the radio were lining up too much to just be random. Since then it’s been there most workdays, in the background at least, and sometimes more intense depending on what happens during the shift.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever considered the possibility that your beliefs about dispatch and compliance watching you might not be true?\n\nPatient: I mean… I’ve tried to consider that, because I know that’s what people keep saying — that dispatch and compliance watching route changes is just normal oversight, that it’s part of the system, that radio comments are just radio comments. And I get that on paper. I’m not saying the computer system isn’t supposed to track things at all.\n\nBut the problem is, it doesn’t feel random to me. It feels too specific. Like I’ll make a route change, and then later there’s some little comment on the radio that sounds normal enough if someone else heard it, but to me it lines up with exactly what I did. And then certain drivers act like they already know something, or they’re watching for something, like they’re reporting back. So when people say, “Maybe you’re reading into it,” I can understand why they’d say that, but I don’t really believe that’s all it is.\n\nThat’s kind of why I’m here, honestly. Because I’m not just walking around thinking everyone is after me. It’s this specific thing with dispatch, compliance, the route system, and the drivers. And even when I try to talk myself out of it, I keep coming back to the timing and the comments and the way people act. It just seems like something is being tracked and passed around about me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that you might be misinterpreting the actions of dispatch and compliance?\n\nPatient: I mean, yes, I can say it’s possible in the abstract. Like, I understand that dispatch has to monitor routes, and compliance probably has access to the computer system for normal reasons. I’m not trying to say there’s no legitimate reason for them to see route changes.\n\nBut when it keeps happening in this pattern, it’s hard for me to believe I’m just misinterpreting it. I’ll change something on my route, and then later there’s a little comment over the radio that sounds like it’s about that exact thing, even if they don’t say my name directly. And then certain drivers will act like they know what happened or like they’re watching to see what I do next. That’s the part that makes it feel like it isn’t just normal oversight.\n\nSo… I can consider that maybe I’m reading too much into some of it, because people keep telling me that. But honestly, I don’t really feel convinced by that explanation. It feels like dispatch and compliance are tracking me more closely than they’re admitting, and that some of the drivers are reporting back on me.\n\nDoctor: If you were shown evidence that dispatch and compliance were not watching you or plotting against you, would you be able to change your mind about what's going on?\n\nPatient: I want to say yes, because I’m not trying to be unreasonable. If someone could actually show me, clearly, that dispatch and compliance weren’t using the computer system to track my route changes in that way, and that the radio comments weren’t connected to me, then… I’d have to look at that.\n\nBut I don’t know what kind of evidence would really prove it, if that makes sense. Because part of the problem is that it’s not like they come out and say, “We’re watching you.” It’s little things. It’s the timing of when I make a change and then later hear something over the radio that sounds like it’s about what I did. Or certain drivers acting like they already know, like they’re checking on me or reporting back. So if someone just showed me a policy that says, “We only use the system for normal oversight,” I don’t think that would settle it for me, because that doesn’t mean people aren’t using it another way.\n\nI guess if there was some real way to see that none of it was being passed around, that no one was looking at my changes except for normal reasons, and those drivers weren’t involved, maybe I could try to change my mind. But honestly, right now it feels very hard to believe that, because it’s been lining up too much. That’s why I’m here — because everyone keeps saying it’s normal, but I can tell there’s more going on than they’re admitting.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself having trouble staying on topic or expressing your thoughts in a coherent manner?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I mean, I don’t think of myself as not coherent. I know I can talk a lot, especially when I’m trying to explain something that has a lot of pieces to it, because if I leave one part out then it sounds different than how it actually happened. So sometimes I’ll go back and add details, or I’ll say, “Wait, that’s not the right order,” and try to explain it again.\n\nBut as far as staying on topic, I guess it depends what you mean. If I’m answering something important, I might circle around it because I’m trying to make sure you understand what I mean, not because I don’t know what I’m saying. Other people might see that as going off topic, maybe. I don’t usually notice it in the moment unless someone points it out or looks impatient.\n\nSo… I’m not sure. I don’t feel like my thoughts are incoherent, but I can admit I probably over-explain things and get caught up in the details.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any other explanation for the events you've described at work, other than dispatch and compliance watching you?\n\nPatient: I’ve tried to think of other explanations, yeah. I mean, I know dispatch has to know where buses are, or where drivers are, and I know compliance has reasons to look at things in the system. So I’m not saying every single time someone checks a route it automatically means something bad.\n\nBut the problem is the way it lines up. Like, if I make a route change or do something a little different, then later there’ll be a comment over the radio that isn’t technically naming me, but it fits too closely. And then certain drivers will act differently around me, like they already heard about it or like they’re waiting to see what I’m going to do next. That’s the part I can’t just explain away as normal work stuff.\n\nI guess another explanation could be coincidence, or people just joking around, or me noticing things more because I’m already stressed about it. I can say that out loud. But it doesn’t feel like that’s what’s happening. It feels like dispatch and compliance are tracking my route changes through the computer system, and that some drivers are reporting back or being told to keep an eye on me.\n\nSo… I can think of other explanations, but I don’t really believe them as much, because the pattern has been too consistent.\n\nDoctor: If someone you trusted completely, like a close family member, told you that they had investigated and found no evidence of dispatch and compliance watching you, would you believe them?\n\nPatient: I would want to believe them. I mean, if it was someone I really trusted, like family, I wouldn’t think they were trying to hurt me or lie to me on purpose. So I’d listen. I’d probably ask them what exactly they looked at, though, because just saying “I didn’t find anything” wouldn’t completely settle it for me.\n\nIt’s hard because the whole reason I’m here is that this has been happening through things that aren’t obvious. Dispatch and compliance aren’t going to put it in writing like, “Yes, we’re tracking her route changes and talking about it over the radio.” It’s more like I’ll make a change in the system, and then later there’s some little radio comment that fits too well, or certain drivers act like they already know what I did, like they’re reporting back or watching to see if I do it again.\n\nSo if my family member said they investigated, I’d probably feel torn. Part of me would think, “Okay, maybe I should calm down and consider that.” But another part of me would wonder if they just weren’t shown the real thing, or if dispatch and compliance made it look normal because that’s what they’re supposed to do. Normal oversight is one thing, but what I’m talking about feels different.\n\nSo… I don’t know if I’d fully believe it right away. I’d take it seriously because I trust them, but I don’t think it would erase what I’ve noticed. Not unless they could really explain the timing, the radio comments, and why certain drivers seem like they’re involved.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that no amount of evidence could ever convince you that dispatch and compliance are not watching you?\n\nPatient: I don’t want to say “no amount,” because that sounds like I’m being unreasonable or like I’ve made up my mind forever and nothing could ever change it. I don’t think of it that way. If someone could actually explain the timing and the radio comments and why certain drivers seem like they already know what I did before I even talk to anyone, then… I mean, I’d have to listen to that.\n\nBut just regular evidence, like someone saying, “That’s normal oversight,” or “Dispatch monitors routes, that’s their job,” that doesn’t really convince me, because I already know they monitor things. That’s part of the issue. I’m saying it goes past normal monitoring. It feels like they’re tracking my route changes through the computer system and then making these little comments over the radio that are indirect, like they’re careful not to say my name but it still matches what I did. And then certain drivers act like they’ve been told something or like they’re reporting back.\n\nSo if the evidence was just, “There’s no official report” or “Compliance says they aren’t watching you,” I don’t think that would be enough. Because of course they wouldn’t put it that way. But if there was some way to really show that those comments weren’t connected, and the drivers weren’t involved, and the computer tracking was only routine and not being used to single me out… then maybe. I’d want that to be true, honestly. I don’t like feeling like this. But right now, from what I’ve seen, it still feels too connected to just ignore.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

    "content": "Return the JSON now."

Completely change my mind… I don’t know. I want to say yes, because I don’t want to be stuck thinking this forever. Like, I would love for someone to show me something that makes all of it make sense in a normal way, because this is exhausting. The part that bothers me is when I make a route change, or something is different in the system, and then later there are these little comments over the radio that don’t say my name, but they line up too closely. And then certain drivers act like they’re watching for what I do next, or like they’re reporting back.\n\nSo if someone could really show me, step by step, that those radio comments weren’t connected to my route changes, and that the drivers weren’t being told anything about me, and that compliance wasn’t using the system to single me out… then maybe I could change my mind. I think I’d need it explained in a way that accounts for the pattern, not just dismisses it.\n\nI guess “completely” is the hard word. I might still feel suspicious at first, even if someone showed me evidence, because it’s been going on in a way that feels so specific. But if they could explain the whole thing clearly, not just tell me I’m wrong, I’d try to believe it. I really would.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

The tracking through the computer system and the radio comments — that’s felt pretty definite for about the past two months.\n\nBefore that, I might have noticed little things here and there and thought, “That was odd,” but I could sort of brush it off or tell myself I was overthinking it. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I wouldn’t say I’m absolutely certain in the sense that nobody could ever tell me anything different. I don’t want to be that kind of person who just refuses to hear anything. But I am pretty convinced, because it’s not just one thing that happened once and I ran with it. It’s the pattern.

Like, I make a route change in the computer system, and then later there’ll be some little comment over the radio that doesn’t say my name, but it fits too closely with what I just did. And then certain drivers will act different, like they already know, or like they’re watching to see if I do it again, or reporting something back. That’s what makes it hard for me to just accept when someone says, “Oh, that’s normal oversight.” I know dispatch and compliance have normal monitoring. I’m not saying they shouldn’t know where routes are. But this feels more personal than that, like I’m being singled out.

So… could I potentially change my mind? Maybe, yes. If someone showed me something that really explained all of that in a way that made sense. Not just, “There’s no evidence,” or, “They said they aren’t doing it,” because that doesn’t answer why the timing keeps lining up or why the comments sound like they’re about my changes. I’d need someone to actually walk through it and show me how it’s not connected.

But right now, with what I’ve seen and heard, I do believe dispatch and compliance are watching me through the system, and that some drivers are reporting on me. I’m not saying I want that to be true. I really don’t. It makes work feel tense all the time. But it feels too specific for me to just drop it because someone tells me it’s routine.

Then it takes too long and I end up behind. Like if we need to schedule something or figure out dinner or call someone back, I’ll think about it and mean to do it, but I don’t get it organized enough to actually finish it. So yes, I guess other people are having trouble understanding me, and I’m having trouble keeping things straight enough to explain them.\n\nDoctor: Are these feelings of being watched and plotted against limited to your work, or do you experience them in other areas of your life as well?\n\nPatient: Mostly it’s work. That’s where I notice it, and that’s what made me come in, because it’s not just a vague feeling like “people are watching me.” It’s very tied to dispatch and compliance and the route system.\n\nLike, I’ll make a route change through the computer because something makes sense in the moment, and then later there’s a comment on the radio that sounds like it’s about that exact thing. Not directly, not like, “We saw you do this,” but in that roundabout way where it’s meant to sound normal if anyone else hears it. And certain drivers seem to know things they shouldn’t really be focused on unless someone is telling them or they’re reporting back. That’s what bothers me.\n\nAt home, I’m not sitting there thinking the neighbors are in on it or that strangers are following me around. It’s not really like that. It’s more that when I leave work, I’m still thinking about what happened there, going over the comments and the timing and wondering what they’re putting together about me. The tracking through the computer system and the radio comments — that’s felt pretty definite for about the past two months.\n\nBefore that, I might have noticed little things here and there and thought, “That was odd,” but I could sort of brush it off or tell myself I was overthinking it. But around two months ago it started feeling more connected, like the route changes and what people were saying on the radio were lining up too much to just be random. It feels like dispatch and compliance are tracking my route changes through the computer system, and that some drivers are reporting back or being told to keep an eye on me.\n\nSo… I can think of other explanations, but I don’t really believe them as much, because the pattern has been too consistent.\n\nDoctor: If someone you trusted completely, like a close family member, told you that they had investigated and found no evidence of dispatch and compliance watching you, would you believe them?\n\nPatient: I would want to believe them.   },

I mean… completely change it? That’s hard for me to answer, because I don’t want to sound like I’m saying nothing could ever change my mind. I’m not trying to be impossible. I don’t like going to work and feeling like every little radio comment might be about me, or like certain drivers are looking at me because they already got told something.\n\nBut it would have to be more than someone just saying, “That’s not happening,” or “Dispatch and compliance are just doing their jobs.” I know they have to monitor routes through the computer system. I know there’s normal oversight. That’s not the part I’m questioning. The part that bothers me is when I make a route change, or something is different in the system, and then later there are these little comments over the radio that don’t say my name, but they line up too closely. And then certain drivers act like they’re watching for what I do next, or like they’re reporting back.\n\nSo if someone could really show me, step by step, that those radio comments weren’t connected to my route changes, and that the drivers weren’t being told anything about me, and that compliance wasn’t using the system to single me out… then maybe I could change my mind. I think I’d need it explained in a way that accounts for the pattern, not just dismisses it.\n\nI guess “completely” is the hard word. I might still feel suspicious at first, even if someone showed me evidence, because it’s been going on in a way that feels so specific. But if they could explain the whole thing clearly, not just tell me I’m wrong, I’d try to believe it. I really would.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your belief about dispatch and compliance watching you is something that you are absolutely certain about, or is it something that you could potentially change your mind about if presented with different information?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say I’m absolutely certain in the sense that nobody could ever tell me anything different. I don’t want to be that kind of person who just refuses to hear anything. But I am pretty convinced, because it’s not just one thing that happened once and I ran with it. It’s the pattern.\n\nLike, I make a route change in the computer system, and then later there’ll be some little comment over the radio that doesn’t say my name, but it fits too closely with what I just did. And then certain drivers will act different, like they already know, or like they’re watching to see if I do it again, or reporting something back. That’s what makes it hard for me to just accept when someone says, “Oh, that’s normal oversight.” I know dispatch and compliance have normal monitoring. I’m not saying they shouldn’t know where routes are. But this feels more personal than that, like I’m being singled out.\n\nSo… could I potentially change my mind? Maybe, yes. If someone showed me something that really explained all of that in a way that made sense. Not just, “There’s no evidence,” or, “They said they aren’t doing it,” because that doesn’t answer why the timing keeps lining up or why the comments sound like they’re about my changes. I’d need someone to actually walk through it and show me how it’s not connected.\n\nBut right now, with what I’ve seen and heard, I do believe dispatch and compliance are watching me through the system, and that some drivers are reporting on me. I’m not saying I want that to be true. I really don’t. It makes work feel tense all the time. But it feels too specific for me to just drop it because someone tells me it’s routine.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that there is anything that could happen, or any information you could receive, that would completely change your belief about dispatch and compliance watching you?\n\nPatient: I mean… completely change it? That’s hard for me to answer, because I don’t want to sound like I’m saying nothing could ever change my mind. I’m not trying to be impossible. But the reason I’m here is because this has felt like more than just regular supervision. Dispatch and compliance have access to the computer system, they can see the route changes, and then I’ll hear these little comments on the radio afterward that seem connected. Not direct, like nobody says, “We’re talking about you,” but close enough that I can tell what it’s about.\n\nAnd people keep saying, “That’s normal oversight,” but that doesn’t really settle it for me, because I know there’s normal oversight. I’m not confused about that part. It’s the way it feels targeted, like when I change something or do something a little different, and then certain drivers seem to know or act like they’re reporting on me. That’s the part I can’t just explain away.\n\nSo if there was information that could really show me the radio comments weren’t about my route changes, and that those drivers weren’t being told anything or sending anything back, and that compliance wasn’t using the system to keep tabs on me specifically… then maybe it could change how I see it. I’d want that, honestly, because it’s stressful walking around feeling watched. But just someone saying, “No, that’s not happening,” probably wouldn’t completely change it. It would have to explain the whole pattern, not just dismiss it.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you often find yourself having trouble putting your thoughts into words when trying to explain something?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item.
